## Q10. For a,b≥1, set A_(a,b)(t)=∑\_(n≥0) ∑\_(k=0)^(n) (binom(n, k))^(a)  (binom(n+k, k))…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 10.5) · **Collection** 1

For a,b≥1, set A_(a,b)(t)=∑\_(n≥0) ∑\_(k=0)^(n) (binom(n, k))^(a)  (binom(n+k, k))^(b)t^(n). Is its minimal rational-diagonal variable count a+b? A diagonal keeps coefficients having equal exponents in all variables.

**Context.** Origin: Joint work with Jakob Steininger, circulated in 2021 and published in 2023 before inclusion in the dissertation.

**Source.** Sergey Yurkevich. *Integer sequences, algebraic series and differential operators*. University of Vienna and Université Paris-Saclay (joint doctorate), 2023. Advisor(s): Alin Bostan; Herwig Hauser. [primary source](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/herwig.hauser/Publications/yurkevich%20dissertation%202023.pdf) · [record](https://efi.math.cnrs.fr/doctorant.e.s.html) Location: Conjecture 10.5, printed p. 167. Follow-on evidence: Bostan–Salvy–Singer (23 April 2025), §5.3, verify the conjecture for all positive a,b with a+b≤10, but retain the general claim. No full proof located.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.vsmath.at/?people=yurkevich-sergey) · [2](https://singer.math.ncsu.edu/papers/BSS.pdf)

## Q57. For sufficiently small ε>0, do constants C_(ε) and an increasing κ\_(ε) exist so …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.0.3) · **Collection** 1

For sufficiently small ε>0, do constants C_(ε) and an increasing κ\_(ε) exist so that |P_(r)(Γ)|≤C_(ε)δ^(−ε)r^(−d−1)|Γ|^(d) whenever Γ consists of ∼W^(d) δ-slabs in [0,1]^(d), 1≤W≤δ^(−1), r≥max{d,δ^(1−ε/4)|Γ|}, and Γ satisfies (WS-2) and κ\_(ε)(W^(−1))-broadness? Here P_(r) counts essentially distinct r-rich δ-boxes; (WS-2) allows at most one Γ-slab essentially inside each W^(−1)-slab in its W^(−1) normal-direction cap; κ-broadness requires every d-rich box to meet d slabs whose common volume is at most δ^(d)/κ. Essential incidence follows the half-volume convention of §1.1.

**Context.** Origin: Tammen proposes this incidence conjecture for her second spacing condition.

**Source.** Sarah Tammen. *Incidence Problems for Slabs*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Larry Guth. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/30df4c87-ea92-4bc8-9c3c-ca91a975130d/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/30df4c87-ea92-4bc8-9c3c-ca91a975130d/content#page=1) Location: Conjecture 2.0.3, p.29; Definition 2.0.2, pp.28–29; (WS-2), p.24. Search-qualified status: The author’s current research page describes her proved result under the first spacing condition; targeted searches found no resolution of the second-condition conjecture.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/sarahtammen/home/research) · [2](https://alexiosevich.com/april2026workshop.html)

## Q124. In a projective plane of order s, suppose a set F of s+1 incident point-line fla…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

In a projective plane of order s, suppose a set F of s+1 incident point-line flags has no common opposite, its point entries do not form a line, and its line entries do not form a line pencil. Must s=2 and F be the three cyclic flags of a triangle in PG(2,2)? Flags (p,L),(q,M) are opposite when p∉M and q∉L.

**Context.** Date note: Dissertation dated December 2025; defense and degree awarded 29 January 2026. Origin: A new conjectural exceptional configuration arising from the thesis’s own hexagon proof; joint research with Hendrik Van Maldeghem, later stated in their 2026 paper. Posed as the remaining octagon case after the dissertation’s own line-size opposition-blocking classification. The associated joint 2026 paper develops and restates the question, rather than attributing it to an older named conjecture.

**Source.** Sira Busch. *Groups of Automorphisms of Spherical Buildings*. University of Münster, 2026. Advisor(s): Linus Kramer. [primary source](https://sirabusch.de/dissertationSiraBusch.pdf) · [record](https://www.uni-muenster.de/MathematicsMuenster/news/artikel/2026/07-03a.shtml) Location: Unnumbered conjecture in Remark 9.9.0.4, printed/PDF p. 308. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The July 30, 2026 revision of Busch–Van Maldeghem arXiv:2602.01110 retains this exact conjecture in Remark 5.4. No subsequent resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01110) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01110) · [3](https://cage.ugent.be/~hvm/artikels/ExceptionalLines_submit.pdf)


<a id="q125"></a>

## Q125. For a finite thick generalized octagon of order (s,t) with s>t, classify all set…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

For a finite thick generalized octagon of order (s,t) with s>t, classify all sets of s+1 points having no common opposite. Here each line has s+1 points, each point lies on t+1 lines, and the incidence graph has diameter 8 and girth 16; two points are opposite when their incidence-graph distance is 8.

**Context.** Date note: Dissertation dated December 2025; defense and degree awarded 29 January 2026. Origin: A new conjectural exceptional configuration arising from the thesis’s own hexagon proof; joint research with Hendrik Van Maldeghem, later stated in their 2026 paper. Posed as the remaining octagon case after the dissertation’s own line-size opposition-blocking classification. The associated joint 2026 paper develops and restates the question, rather than attributing it to an older named conjecture.

**Source.** Sira Busch. *Groups of Automorphisms of Spherical Buildings*. University of Münster, 2026. Advisor(s): Linus Kramer. [primary source](https://sirabusch.de/dissertationSiraBusch.pdf) · [record](https://www.uni-muenster.de/MathematicsMuenster/news/artikel/2026/07-03a.shtml) Location: Note 9.9.0.5, printed/PDF p. 308. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: July 2026 arXiv:2602.01110, Remarks 5.10–5.11, still identifies the Moufang octagon case of order (s,√s) as open, while the (s,s²) case has only lines. Thus even that special case of the dissertation’s s>t question remains unresolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01110) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01110) · [3](https://cage.ugent.be/~hvm/artikels/ExceptionalLines_submit.pdf)

## Q126. For every Λ, does a geodesic Λ-metric space exist whose segments sharing an endp…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.3) · **Collection** 2

For every Λ, does a geodesic Λ-metric space exist whose segments sharing an endpoint intersect in a segment, but which violates the segment-union axiom?

**Context.** Origin: The metric questions originate in the author’s 2021/2024 paper; the building question extends his thesis construction. Setup for question 126: Λ is a nonzero ordered abelian group. A Λ-metric satisfies the usual metric axioms with values in Λ; a segment is an isometric image of a closed Λ-interval. Axiom (2) requires segments meeting only at a common endpoint to have segment union.

**Source.** Raphael Appenzeller. *Semialgebraic groups and generalized affine buildings*. ETH Zürich, 2024. Advisor(s): Marc Burger. [primary source](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/server/api/core/bitstreams/5ce6ff77-8828-4836-a97b-282442c2da87/content) · [record](https://math.ethz.ch/news-and-events/news/d-math-news/2024/07/doctoral-exam-of-raphael-appenzeller.html) Location: Question 3.3, printed p. 17 / arXiv thesis PDF p. 23; definitions pp. 16–17. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The 2024 published article retains them as Questions 1–2. Positive answers are known when Λ≠2Λ or Λ is the additive group of an ordered field; Λ=Z[1/2] is a highlighted unresolved case. Searches and the author’s 2026 publication list revealed no general resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~rappenzeller/) · [2](https://www.degruyterbrill.com/document/doi/10.1515/agms-2023-0106/html) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2112.02704)

## Q127. Let G⊂SL_n be a self-adjoint semisimple algebraic group over K⊂R, and F⊃K real c…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.62) · **Collection** 2

Let G⊂SL_n be a self-adjoint semisimple algebraic group over K⊂R, and F⊃K real closed with an order-compatible valuation v:F×→Λ. On X=G(F)·Id use d=−v∘N∘δ, where δ is the Cartan projection and N(a)=∏\_(α∈Σ)max{χα(a),χα(a)⁻¹}. Is B=X/{d=0}, with apartments the G(F)-translates of the split-torus orbit, an affine Λ-building when the restricted root system Σ is nonreduced?

**Context.** Origin: The metric questions originate in the author’s 2021/2024 paper; the building question extends his thesis construction.

**Source.** Raphael Appenzeller. *Semialgebraic groups and generalized affine buildings*. ETH Zürich, 2024. Advisor(s): Marc Burger. [primary source](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/server/api/core/bitstreams/5ce6ff77-8828-4836-a97b-282442c2da87/content) · [record](https://math.ethz.ch/news-and-events/news/d-math-news/2024/07/doctoral-exam-of-raphael-appenzeller.html) Location: Question 8.62, printed p. 121 / arXiv thesis PDF p. 127; construction §§1.2–1.3 and 7–8. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Explicitly remains Question 5.63 in the 2026 paper Generalized affine buildings for semisimple algebraic groups over real closed fields, accepted by Münster Journal of Mathematics. Its Theorem 5.1 still assumes reduced roots; the unqualified abstract must not be read as resolving the question.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~rappenzeller/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.03226)

## Q188. Fix 2 < s < 4. Let E be a collection of ≍ δ^(−s) balls of radius δ in the unit b…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1.2) · **Collection** 2

Fix 2 < s < 4. Let E be a collection of ≍ δ^(−s) balls of radius δ in the unit ball of ℂ², with O(1) members in every ball of radius δ^(s/4). For Δ(E) = {(x₁ − x₂)² + (y₁ − y₂)² : (x_(i), y_(i)) ∈ ⋃E}, must its covering number satisfy N_(δ)(Δ(E)) ≳ δ⁻², with no δ^(ε) loss?

**Context.** Origin: The specific well-spaced discretized formulation belongs to Zhang’s joint complex-tube work with Sarah Tammen (2022 preprint, disclosed in thesis abstract). Section3.1 introduces this formulation and Conjecture 3.1.2; p. 36 expressly distinguishes its conjectured bound from the epsilon-loss result proved there. This is not the broad older Falconer conjecture.

**Source.** Lingxian Zhang. *Application of high-low method to distance problems*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Larry Guth. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/293aad9e-f29c-4494-b352-9d86c0fc06b9/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/293aad9e-f29c-4494-b352-9d86c0fc06b9/content#page=1) Location: Conjecture 3.1.2, printed pp. 21–22; comparison with partial result on p. 36. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The joint paper published on 20 April 2026 still proves only N_delta >= c_(epsilon,s,N) delta^(-2+epsilon) (Theorem 5.2, p. 15), not the conjectured delta^(-2) bound. Exact author/topic and epsilon-removal searches on 2026-10-05 found no later resolution. Openness assessment rests on the explicit gap, not on the paper’s abstract phrase that a discretized variant is resolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://ems.press/journals/jfg/articles/14299674) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.05477)

## Q194. For 0 ≤ d < n, is UW_d([0,1]^n) = √(n−d) for the Euclidean unit cube? Here UW_d(…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

For 0 ≤ d < n, is UW_d([0,1]^n) = √(n−d) for the Euclidean unit cube? Here UW_d(X) = inf_f sup_y diam_X(f⁻¹(y)), over continuous maps to simplicial complexes of dimension at most d.

**Context.** Origin: The author explicitly proposes the interpolating formula after computing the known endpoint widths, and states that he cannot prove it. It is not attributed to an older conjecture. Original quantitative refinement from Balitskiy’s joint work with Aleksandr Berdnikov: Question 5.1 of their 2020 preprint, after their upper bound and lower-bound constructions; reproduced in the thesis.

**Source.** Alexey Balitskiy. *Bounds on Urysohn width*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2021. Advisor(s): Larry Guth. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/1535c080-2f5c-47ef-89f1-ba58dd938926/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/1535c080-2f5c-47ef-89f1-ba58dd938926/content#page=1) Location: Unnumbered conjecture in §2.5, printed p. 37. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The thesis leaves intermediate dimensions open. Exact formula/author searches and current publication list revealed no resolution. Checked 2026 work on macroscopic curvature and surgeries; those results concern manifold width and do not establish this exact cube formula.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2008.07718) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.14669) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.20522) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15565) · [5](https://balit.ski/papers)

## Q195. For fixed sufficiently small ε > 0, determine the optimal growth of f(β) such th…

For fixed sufficiently small ε > 0, determine the optimal growth of f(β) such that every closed Riemannian surface M with β = dim H₁(M; ℤ/2) and UW₁(B) < ε for all unit balls satisfies UW₁(M) ≲ f(β). Width is the infimum of maximum fiber diameter over continuous maps to graphs, with ambient distances used on each ball.

Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The source bounds the optimal dependence between square-root and linear growth in beta. Related 2026 papers concern other macroscopic-curvature/surgery hypotheses and do not close this surface local-width gap. Targeted searches found no later resolution.

## Q206. Do the regular icosahedron and dodecahedron both have Nieuwland number equal to …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Do the regular icosahedron and dodecahedron both have Nieuwland number equal to the root near \$1.0108\$ of \$2025x^8-11970x^6+17009x^4-9000x^2+2000\$? Here \$\nu(P)\$ is the supremum of scale factors \$\mu\$ for which a planar projection of \$\mu P\$ fits strictly inside another projection of \$P\$, allowing planar rigid motions.

**Context.** Origin: Joint work with Jakob Steininger, circulated in 2021 and published in 2023 before inclusion in the dissertation.

## Q377. Let \$\Theta\subset\ell_2\$ be compact and convex, invariant under coordinate sign…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Let \$\Theta\subset\ell_2\$ be compact and convex, invariant under coordinate sign changes, and such that \$\\{(\theta_i^2)\_i:\theta\in\Theta\\}\$ is convex. Set \$D(\Theta,t)=\min\\{d\ge0:\inf_{\dim L=d}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\operatorname{dist}(\theta,L)\le t\\}\$. Do universal constants \$c_1,c_2>0\$ exist such that, for every \$\varepsilon>0\$, \$\Theta\$ contains \$D(\Theta,c_1\varepsilon)\$ pairwise orthogonal vectors, each of norm \$c_2\varepsilon\$? This uses the source prose’s minimum-dimension convention; see the editorial note.

## Q396. If \$D\$ is free and \$J_h\$ is radical, must the normalization \$\pi:\widetilde D\to…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.51) · **Collection** 4

If \$D\$ is free and \$J_h\$ is radical, must the normalization \$\pi:\widetilde D\to D\$ be Gorenstein at every point over the origin?

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Sergey Yurkevich → Herwig Hauser: https://homepage.univie.ac.at/herwig.hauser/Publications/yurkevich%20dissertation%202023.pdf
Eleonore Faber → Herwig Hauser: https://utheses.univie.ac.at/detail/15185 Origin: Author’s labelled questions following her new Jacobian-ideal results. Shared setup for questions 396, 397, 398: Let \$\mathcal O=\mathbb C\\{x_1,\ldots,x_n\\}\$, let \$h\in\mathcal O\$ be reduced, and set \$D=\\{h=0\\}\$, \$J_h=(\partial h/\partial x_1,\ldots,\partial h/\partial x_n)\$. The divisor is free when \$\operatorname{Der}\_{\mathcal O}(-\log D)\$ is a free \$\mathcal O\$-module; equidimensional means all minimal components have equal dimension.

**Source.** Eleonore Faber. *Normal crossings in local analytic geometry*. University of Vienna, 2011. Advisor(s): Herwig Hauser. [primary source](https://imsc.uni-graz.at/faber/files/diss_a5_oct11.pdf) · [record](https://utheses.univie.ac.at/detail/15185) Location: Question 2.51, printed p. 86 / PDF p. 100.

## Q1120. Hausdorff threshold for displacement energy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For a given symplectic manifold (M²ⁿ,ω), determine inf{dim_H A:A⊂M, e(A)>0}. Here e(A)=inf_H∫₀¹(sup_M H_t−inf_M H_t)dt over smooth Hamiltonians with every time-t flow a global diffeomorphism and φ\_H¹(A)∩A=∅; compact support is not imposed. Hausdorff dimension is computed with any smooth Riemannian metric.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1120, 1121: Earlier Guggisberg–Ziltener 2024 joint-paper questions; restated in thesis. https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17444

**Source.** Yann Bertrand Guggisberg. *Recognition through and representability of symplectic capacities and squeezing of small sets*. 2025. Advisor(s): Fabian Ziltener; statutory promotor: Marius Crainic. [primary source](https://research-portal.uu.nl/ws/files/271426916/phd%20thesis%20yann%20guggisberg%20-%2068bd2800d3b7a.pdf) Location: Q5.1.4; Def.2.1.3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1120, 1121, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: rectifiable variants solved; unrestricted targets remain unreported as resolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17444) · [2](https://doi.org/10.33540/3064)


<a id="q1121"></a>

## Q1121. Hausdorff threshold for symplectic squeezing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For n≥3, determine inf{dim_H A:A⊂R²ⁿ is not arbitrarily symplectically squeezable}. Squeezable means: for every ε>0, there is a map A→B²ⁿ\_ε injective on A that extends to a symplectic map on an open neighborhood of A. No boundedness or rectifiability assumption is imposed.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1120, 1121: Earlier Guggisberg–Ziltener 2024 joint-paper questions; restated in thesis. https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17444

**Source.** Yann Bertrand Guggisberg. *Recognition through and representability of symplectic capacities and squeezing of small sets*. 2025. Advisor(s): Fabian Ziltener; statutory promotor: Marius Crainic. [primary source](https://research-portal.uu.nl/ws/files/271426916/phd%20thesis%20yann%20guggisberg%20-%2068bd2800d3b7a.pdf) Location: Q5.1.13; Def.5.1.10.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1120, 1121, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: rectifiable variants solved; unrestricted targets remain unreported as resolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17444) · [2](https://doi.org/10.33540/3064)


<a id="q1122"></a>

## Q1122. Exact instability of the Foscolo–Haskins nearly Kähler metric

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For the inhomogeneous cohomogeneity-one nearly Kähler structure on S³×S³ constructed by Foscolo–Haskins, are the lower bounds Hitchin index≥1 and Einstein co-index≥4 sharp? Normalize Ric=5g. The Hitchin index is Σ\_{0<λ<12}dim{β primitive real (1,1):d\*β=0, Δβ=λβ}; the Einstein co-index counts positive directions of the normalized Einstein–Hilbert Hessian on transverse-traceless tensors.

**Context.** Origin for question 1122: Thesis explicitly asks sharpness after its lower-bound theorem.

**Source.** Enric Solé-Farré. *Conical singularities in special holonomy and gauge theory*. 2025. Advisor(s): Simon Donaldson; Lorenzo Foscolo. [primary source](https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/id/eprint/10212567/12/Sole%20Farre_10212567_Thesis.pdf) Location: p.10; Def.7.17; Thm.12.6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1122, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: no sharp-index result found; associated preprint corrections preserve lower bounds. https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21106

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21106) · [2](https://enric-sf.github.io/) · [3](https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/id/eprint/10212567/)


<a id="q1123"></a>

## Q1123. A unique least-drift hyperbolic structure

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

Let S be a closed oriented surface of genus≥2 and μ a probability measure whose support generates π₁(S) as a semigroup, with ∑\_g μ(g)|g|<∞ for a finite generating-set word metric |·|. For iid μ-increments let Z_n=g₁⋯g_n and ℓ\_μ(ρ)=lim_n |Z_n|\_ρ/n, where |g|\_ρ is the hyperbolic length of the free homotopy class of g. Does ℓ\_μ attain its minimum at exactly one point of Teichmüller space T(S)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1123: Restates Question 5.4 in Random walk speed is a proper function on Teichmüller space, Azemar–Gadre–Gouëzel–Haettel–Lessa–Uyanik (2023). https://arxiv.org/pdf/2212.06581

**Source.** Aitor Azemar. *Asymptotic geometric and probabilistic properties of Teichmüller space*. 2024. Advisor(s): Vaibhav Gadre; Tara Brendle; Maxime Fortier-Bourque. [primary source](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84802/1/2024AzemarPhD.pdf) Location: Q4.7.3, p.67.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1123, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: no uniqueness theorem found. The 2026 singularity theorem is distinct. https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19115

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2212.06581) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19115) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.19115v1) · [4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.04627) · [5](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84802/)


<a id="q1124"></a>

## Q1124. Systoles of dynamically convex domains

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For every smooth uniformly star-shaped dynamically convex domain X⊂R⁴, must each least-action Reeb orbit on ∂X be a Hopf orbit, hence bound a disk-like global surface of section? Hopf means unknotted with transverse self-linking −1; dynamical convexity means every closed orbit has lower-semicontinuous Conley–Zehnder index≥3 in the standard trivialization.

**Context.** Origin for question 1124: Hofer–Wysocki–Zehnder question, thesis-restated; retain dynamically convex case.

**Source.** Oliver Edtmair. *Some quantitative results in symplectic geometry*. 2024. Advisor(s): Michael Hutchings. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt4g50539m/qt4g50539m_noSplash_dd7b8be5b8d17706d146ad141cf8ff0e.pdf) Location: Q2.1.10, p.56.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1124, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: uniformly convex case proved; dynamical-convexity extension left open. The 2026 capacity identification does not settle minimum action. https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01777 https://doi.org/10.1112/topo.70055

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01777) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1112/topo.70055) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2412.01777v1) · [4](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1112/topo.70055) · [5](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4g50539m)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

Does every doubly degenerate hyperbolic 3-manifold with unbounded geometry contain a complete embedded minimal surface of finite area? Unbounded geometry means that injectivity radii are not uniformly bounded away from zero.

## Q1129. Equivariant symplectic Fano realization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

Is every compact connected monotone symplectic six-manifold (M,ω) with an effective Hamiltonian GKM T²-action T²-equivariantly symplectomorphic to a smooth Fano threefold with an invariant Kähler form and a holomorphic T²-action? Here c₁(M)=r[ω] with r>0. GKM requires finitely many fixed points and a one-skeleton formed by invariant 2-spheres.

**Context.** Origin for question 1129: Authors’ conjecture; paper-origin attribution.

**Source.** Isabelle Charton; Liat Kessler. *Monotone Symplectic Six-Manifolds That Admit a Hamiltonian GKM Action are Diffeomorphic to Smooth Fano Threefolds*. 2025. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00031-025-09914-2) Location: Conj.1.9.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1129, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: diffeomorphism proved; equivariant symplectomorphism remains conjectured.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00031-025-09914-2)


<a id="q1133"></a>

## Q1133. A symmetry threshold for positive intermediate Ricci curvature

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For each integer k≥1, does there exist an integer d(k), independent of dimension, such that every closed even-dimensional Riemannian manifold Mⁿ, n>k, with Ric_k>0 and an effective isometric T^{d(k)}-action has χ(M)>0? Here Ric_k>0 means Σ\_{i=1}^k sec(v,e_i)>0 for every orthonormal (k+1)-frame (v,e₁,…,e_k).

**Context.** Origin for question 1133: Authors’ introductory question.

**Source.** Lee Kennard; Lawrence Mouillé; Jan Nienhaus. *On Hopf’s conjecture and positive second intermediate Ricci curvature*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.16936v1) Location: Introduction, after known Ric_k examples.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1133, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: no symmetry-threshold resolution found. The September Hopf counterexample claim lacks arbitrarily high torus symmetry. https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11980

## Q1219. Extremal projective tori are monotone

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1.6) · **Collection** 13

For n≥3, must every extremal Lagrangian torus in (CPⁿ,ω\_FS) be monotone? The converse is known.

**Context.** Origin for question 1219: Cieliebak–Mohnke conjecture, restated in thesis. Setup for question 1219: For closed Lagrangian L⊂(X,ω), A_min(L)=inf{∫\_Aω>0:A∈π₂(X,L)}. An extremal torus maximizes A_min over all Lagrangian tori in X. Monotone means [ω]=κμ\_L on π₂(X,L) for some κ>0.

**Source.** Shah Faisal. *Extremal Lagrangian tori in toric domains*. 2024. Advisor(s): Klaus Mohnke. [primary source](https://edoc.hu-berlin.de/bitstreams/c5a7cb95-49b3-4ef7-bd63-68ec6d75ac4a/download) Location: Conjecture 1.1.6, p. 12; Faisal–Li 2026 Conjecture 5.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.6) · **Collection** 13

## Q1224. A first variation for the Lagrangian bidisk

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.13) · **Collection** 13

For P_L={(x,y)∈R²×R²:|x|≤1,|y|≤1}, does a smooth path φ^t∈Symp with φ^0=id exist such that (d/dt)|₀ M(φ^t(P_L))≠0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1223, 1224: Doctoral research connection; use corrected 2026 statements. Setup for questions 1223, 1224: M(K)=∫\_{S^{2 n−1}}(h_K(u)+h_K(−u))dσ(u), with h_K(u)=sup_{x∈K}⟨x,u⟩ and σ normalized spherical measure. Symp is the global standard symplectomorphism group; toric means invariant under coordinatewise complex rotations.

**Source.** Jonghyeon Ahn; Ely Kerman. *Symplectic variations of convex bodies and the mean width*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2311.02870v3) Location: Question 1.13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1223, 1224, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: global proof withdrawn; both questions remain explicit.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02870) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.07220) · [3](https://www.ideals.illinois.edu/items/136467)


<a id="q1226"></a>

## Q1226. Most-volume uniqueness without curvature decay

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 13

For every complete smooth Riemannian surface with nonnegative Gaussian curvature, Euclidean volume growth, and not isometric to R², does there exist measurable G⊂(0,∞) with lim_{r→∞}|G∩(r,2 r)|/r=1 such that every V∈G has a unique isoperimetric set whose boundary is smooth and strictly volume-preserving stable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1226, 1227: Authors’ questions, first 2024; journal 2025. Setup for questions 1226, 1227: Euclidean volume growth means AVR(M)=lim_{r→∞}Vol(B_r(p))/(ω\_n r^n)>0. Quadratic curvature decay means |Rm(x)|≤C/d(x,p)². Isoperimetric sets minimize perimeter at fixed volume.

**Source.** Gioacchino Antonelli; Marco Pozzetta; Daniele Semola. *Uniqueness on average of large isoperimetric sets in noncompact manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.07509v2) Location: Conjecture 1.3 with Theorem 1.1; §1.3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1226, 1227, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: retained 2025; no later resolution found.

<a id="q1232"></a>

## Q1232. Right rigidity without left rigidity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 13

Does an RGR group which is not LGR exist?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1232, 1233: Authors’ questions, first 2024. Setup for questions 1232, 1233: All groups are finitely generated and residually finite. An injection A→B is a Grothendieck pair if it induces an isomorphism of profinite completions. A group is LGR (respectively RGR) if every such injection with it as domain (respectively codomain) is surjective.

**Source.** Andrei Jaikin-Zapirain; Alexander Lubotzky. *Some remarks on Grothendieck pairs*. 2025. [primary source](https://andreijaikin.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/grothendieckpairs2024091.pdf) Location: Question 2, p. 7.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

## Q1431. Torsion-freeness of all Artin groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.2) · **Collection** 15

Is every such A torsion-free?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1431, 1432, 1433: Classical Artin-group questions, restated in this paper. Setup for questions 1431, 1432, 1433: For finite S and symmetric m_st∈{2,3,…,∞}, let A=〈S | alternating words sts…=tst… of length m_st, when m_st<∞〉. Let W=A/〈s²:s∈S〉. Irreducible means S has no nontrivial partition with every cross coefficient 2.

## Q1432. The nonspherical Artin center conjecture

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.2) · **Collection** 15

If A is irreducible and W is infinite, must Z(A)={1}?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1431, 1432, 1433: Classical Artin-group questions, restated in this paper. Setup for questions 1431, 1432, 1433: For finite S and symmetric m_st∈{2,3,…,∞}, let A=〈S | alternating words sts…=tst… of length m_st, when m_st<∞〉. Let W=A/〈s²:s∈S〉. Irreducible means S has no nontrivial partition with every cross coefficient 2.

**Source.** Antoine Goldsborough and Nicolas Vaskou. *Random Artin groups*. 2025. [primary source](https://msp.org/agt/2025/25-3/agt-v25-n3-p07-s.pdf) Location: Conjecture 2.2(2).

## Q1518. Round Yamabe values for lens spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.1.1) · **Collection** 16

For coprime integers p≥3 and q, is σ(L(p,q))=σ(S³)/p^(2/3), where L(p,q)=S³/((z₁,z₂)∼(e^(2πi/p)z₁,e^(2πiq/p)z₂))?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1518, 1519: Schoen’s older spherical-space-form conjecture, restated in the thesis. Setup for questions 1518, 1519: For a closed n-manifold, σ(M)=sup_[g] inf_{h∈[g]}(∫\_M Scal_h dvol_h)/Vol_h(M)^((n−2)/n). Sⁿ has its standard smooth structure.

**Source.** Xuan Yao. *Scalar Curvature and its Topological Implications*. 2025. Advisor(s): Xin Zhou. [primary source](https://ecommons.cornell.edu/bitstreams/3ed99672-54b8-4a8e-8213-d2fddc0b8808/download) Location: Conjecture 2.1.1, printed p. 19.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1518, 1519, checked 6 October 2026: thesis and subsequent-topic searches checked; no resolutions found. The known RP³ value does not settle these regimes.

## Q1522. Rational stable commutator length in surface groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 16

For every closed connected orientable surface S of genus≥2 and g∈[π₁S,π₁S], is scl(g) rational?

**Context.** Origin for question 1522: The older surface-group rationality problem is restated in the dissertation. Setup for question 1522: For g∈[G,G], cl(g) is the least number of commutators multiplying to g and scl(g)=lim_{k→∞}cl(g^k)/k.

**Source.** Alexis Marchand. *Computations and lower bounds for scl and the relative Gromov seminorm*. 2024. Advisor(s): Henry Wilton. [primary source](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/bitstreams/41b48a51-0430-4cba-9262-44b23c939a40/download) Location: Introduction pp. 4,9–10; §I.1.a.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1522, checked 6 October 2026: Later primary papers still describe surface-group rationality as open; no subsequent resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://ems.press/journals/lem/articles/14298067) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.14009v3) · [3](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/proceedings-of-the-royal-society-of-edinburgh-section-a-mathematics/article/on-stable-commutator-length-of-nonfilling-curves-in-surfaces/88C5BA3D19C6D336E9A53BAA865A7D9A) · [4](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/70e8b92a-d7a0-4d58-9ea2-1e3a9749fa53) · [5](https://doi.org/10.17863/CAM.112418)

## Q1523. Finite generation of multicurve stabilizers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 16

For every finite collection σ of disjoint, pairwise nonisotopic essential simple closed curves on ∂V_g, is its setwise stabilizer in Mod(V_g) finitely generated?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1523, 1524, 1525, 1526: Problems 1,3,24 are compiled here; Problem 4 is due to Ng–Schleimer. Setup for questions 1523, 1524, 1525, 1526: V_g is the compact orientable genus-g handlebody, g≥2; Mod(V_g) denotes orientation-preserving mapping classes. Disc-graph vertices are isotopy classes of essential properly embedded discs, adjacent for disjoint representatives; edges have length one. No spots or punctures are marked.

**Source.** Naomi Andrew, Sebastian Hensel, Sam Hughes and Richard D. Wade. *Problems on handlebody groups*. 2025. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/199093/1/rsos.250502.pdf) Location: Problem 1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1523, 1524, 1525, 1526, checked 6 October 2026: published 2026 text retains these problems; later exact-topic searches found no resolutions.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1098/rsos.250502)

## Q1531. Contractible almost-existence in cotangent bundles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 16

For every closed connected non-aspherical manifold Q and proper smooth Hamiltonian H:T\*Q→R having a closed connected regular level H⁻¹(c), is there ε>0 such that almost every regular energy value c′∈(c−ε,c+ε) has a contractible periodic orbit on H⁻¹(c′)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1531: Conjecture 1.2; reaffirmed in the 2026 Heidelberg problem list. Setup for question 1531: T\*Q carries dλ. A non-aspherical Q is one with noncontractible universal cover. “Almost every” refers to Lebesgue measure on energy values; periodic orbits must be nonconstant and contractible in T\*Q.

**Source.** Jean-François Barraud, Mihai Damian, Vincent Humilière and Alexandru Oancea. *Floer Homology with DG Coefficients*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2404.07953v3) Location: Definition 1.1 and Conjecture 1.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1531, checked 6 October 2026: May 2026 final revision retains Conjecture 1.2; its theorems constrain the hypersurface or base. No later full resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.12896v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07953)


<a id="q1617"></a>

## Q1617. Non-σ-compact boundary from finite presentation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1.2) · **Collection** 17

Does there exist a finitely presented group G for which ∂\*G is not σ-compact?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1617, 1618: Finite-presentation question: He–Spriano–Zbinden (2024), Question 2. Quasi-isometry question: Dahmani–Guirardel–Osin (2017), Problem 9.1; Osin (2018), Question 2.20(a). Setup for questions 1617, 1618: The Morse boundary ∂\*G uses Morse geodesic rays in a finite-generator Cayley graph, modulo finite Hausdorff distance, with the direct-limit topology over Morse gauges. σ-compact means a countable union of compact subsets. Morse rays uniformly control quasi-geodesics with endpoints on them. An action is acylindrical if, for each ε>0, some R,N bound by N the elements moving both x,y by ≤ε whenever d(x,y)≥R; non-elementary means at least three boundary limit points.

**Source.** Stefanie Zbinden. *(Non)-Morse directions in groups*. 2025. Advisor(s): Alessandro Sisto. [primary source](https://www.ros.hw.ac.uk/bitstreams/e1a9133b-85a5-4d47-8140-d067c8402a54/download) Location: Question 5.1.2, p. 122.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1617, 1618, checked 6 October 2026: later equivalence results do not settle finite presentation; bounded searches found neither target resolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mathunion.org/fileadmin/ICM/Proceedings/uploads/ICM-2018-vol2-ver1-eb.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18863) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11126) · [4](https://doi.org/10.4153/S0008439523000012) · [5](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.11126v1) · [6](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21325) · [7](https://www.stefaniezbinden.ch/) · [8](https://alexsisto.net/) · [9](https://doi.org/10.1515/jgth-2024-0133) · [10](https://hdl.handle.net/10399/5200)

## Q1619. Hidden flow symmetries force hyperbolicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.1.3) · **Collection** 17

Let dim M≥3 and −4&lt;K≤−1. If finite Riemannian covers of M admit infinitely many centralizer elements, modulo the flow, that do not lift from other covers, must M be homothetic to a real hyperbolic manifold?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1619, 1620: Both targets appear earlier in Mitsutani, Symmetries of geodesic flows on covers and rigidity (2024), §1.1. https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09576 Setup for questions 1619, 1620: For a closed connected negatively curved Riemannian M, SM denotes its unit tangent bundle and φ\_t its geodesic flow; a centralizer element is a smooth diffeomorphism ψ with ψφ\_t=φ\_tψ.

**Source.** Daniel Mitsutani. *Problems at the Intersection of Geometry and Dynamics: Lyapunov Exponents, Rigidity and Entropy of Geodesic Flows*. 2025. Advisor(s): Amie Wilkinson. ## Q1622. Empty normal-subgroup Morse boundary

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.5) · **Collection** 17

Do there exist infinite finitely generated groups H◁G such that ∂\*H=∅ but ∂\*G≠∅?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1621, 1622: The authors’ Questions 1.3 and 1.5; preprint 2024, journal 2025. Setup for questions 1621, 1622: Groups are finitely generated. ∂\* uses the direct-limit topology on Morse-ray strata. A right-angled Artin group has a finite graph presentation with generators the vertices and commutation relations along edges.

**Source.** Ruth Charney, Matthew Cordes, Antoine Goldsborough, Alessandro Sisto and Stefanie Zbinden. *(Non-)existence of Cannon–Thurston maps for Morse boundaries*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/blms.13207) Location: Question 1.5, §1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1621, 1622, checked 6 October 2026: November 2024 v2 and journal checked; bounded later searches found no answers.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17572) · [2](https://www.stefaniezbinden.ch/) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.11126v1)


<a id="q1623"></a>

## Q1623. Quadratic filling forces Morse local-to-global

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 17

Does every finitely presented group with quadratic Dehn function satisfy MLTG in its Cayley graph?

**Context.** Origin for question 1623: Russell–Spriano–Tran (2022), Question 1, already asks the quadratic-Dehn case; reaffirmed here. Setup for question 1623: A Q-quasi-geodesic is M-Morse if each P-quasi-geodesic between its points stays within M(P) of the intervening subpath. MLTG means: for every Q≥1 and Morse gauge M, some L,Q′,M′ make every L-locally M-Morse Q-quasi-geodesic globally M′-Morse and Q′-quasi-geodesic. Here L-local means that every restriction to a parameter interval of length at most L has the property.

**Source.** Carolyn Abbott and Stefanie Zbinden. *Connections between the topology of the Morse boundary, the Morse local-to-global property and acylindrical hyperbolicity*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.11126v1) Location: Question 2, §1.2; Definition 2.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1623, checked 6 October 2026: v1, 11 May 2026, retains the target; exact-topic searches found no later resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/196562/1/s00209-021-02811-w.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11126)

## Q1632. Capacity bounded by normalized surface area

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.9) · **Collection** 17

For every n≥2 and convex body K⊂R^{2 n}, is c_EH(K)≤(Area(∂K)/Area(∂B))^{2/(2 n−1)}, where B is the Euclidean unit ball and c_EH(B)=1?

**Context.** Origin for question 1632: Explicit surface-area version following the authors’ Question 2.9. Setup for question 1632: c_EH is the first Ekeland–Hofer capacity on standard symplectic R^{2n}, normalized by c_EH(B)=1 for the Euclidean unit ball B.

**Source.** Jonghyeon Ahn; Ely Kerman. *On the symplectic capacity and mean width of convex bodies*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.07220v1) Location: The explicit “most ambitious version” immediately following Question 2.9.

<a id="q1725"></a>

## Q1725. Ricci bounds on ramified orientation covers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

For n≥4, is the ramified orientable double cover of every nonorientable noncollapsed RCD(−(n−1),n) space without boundary again RCD(−(n−1),n)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1725: Authors’ §1.6 question. Setup for question 1725: RCD(K,n) denotes the infinitesimally Hilbertian synthetic curvature-dimension condition. Noncollapsed means reference measure H^n. For a nonorientable such space without boundary, its ramified orientable double cover is the metric completion of the ordinary orientation cover of its effective manifold part, with lifted length metric (Theorem 3.1).

**Source.** Camillo Brena, Elia Bruè and Alessandro Pigati. *Lower Ricci Curvature Bounds and the Orientability of Spaces*. 2024. [primary source](https://poisson.phc.dm.unipi.it/~pigati/papers/orientability.pdf) Location: §1.6 unnumbered Question, p. 6; construction Theorem 3.1, pp. 9–10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1725, checked 6 October 2026: n=3 is solved by Deng–Pigati; the higher-dimensional nonsmoothable case remains in the checked literature.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19288) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21961) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00526-026-03297-2) · [4](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00526-026-03297-2#Sec31)


<a id="q1726"></a>

## Q1726. Smoothability of synthetic Ricci three-manifolds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3) · **Collection** 18

Is every RCD(−2,3) space that is a topological 3-manifold a noncollapsed Ricci limit of complete smooth 3-manifolds?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1726, 1727: Smoothability originates in Bruè–Pigati–Semola (2024); compactification is Question 5.6 here. Setup for questions 1726, 1727: RCD(K,n) uses H^n and the infinitesimally Hilbertian curvature-dimension condition. Noncollapsed Ricci limits use pointed GH convergence, uniformly lower Ricci curvature and positive unit-ball-volume bounds. Euclidean growth means liminf_{r→∞}vol(B_r)/r⁴>0; blow-downs are pointed limits of (M,r_i⁻²g), r_i→∞.

**Source.** Daniele Semola. *The large scale structure of complete 4-manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature and Euclidean volume growth*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07125) Location: Question 5.3, p. 32.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1726, 1727, checked 6 October 2026: bounded subsequent searches found no resolution of these specific targets.

## Q1818. Nonunique nonnegative-scalar Ricci expanders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Can two nonisometric gradient expanders of nonnegative scalar curvature on the same Int(X) have the same smooth asymptotic cone γ of nonnegative scalar curvature?

**Context.** Origin for question 1818: Bamler–Chen (2023), Question 1.18; retained in this thesis. Setup for question 1818: X is a compact smooth four-orbifold with isolated singularities, regular nonempty boundary admitting positive scalar curvature, and a manifold orbifold cover X̂ with H₂(X̂; Z)=0 and torsion-free H₁(X̂; Z). Expanders are complete, smooth and satisfy Ric_g+∇²f+g/2=0. Asymptotic to γ=dr²+r²h means rʲ|∇ʲ\_γ(g−γ)|\_γ→0 for j=0,1,2 in end coordinates with ∇f=−r∂r/2.

**Source.** Abishek Rajan. *Expanding Ricci Solitons and the Expander Degree*. 2026. Advisor(s): Richard Bamler. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt0005n3dd/qt0005n3dd.pdf) Location: §4.1, pp. 63–64.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1818, checked 6 October 2026: nonunique nonnegative-scalar examples remain unknown.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03154) · [2](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s12220-026-02593-9) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1002/cpa.70024) · [4](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/0005n3dd)

## Q1833. Inner rolling under upper curvature bounds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 19

Let M^m be simply connected, m≥2, sec≤c. Let D be a convex domain with smooth connected boundary, compactly contained in a ball of radius inj(M)/2. Inward principal curvatures are ≤λ, where λ>0 and, if c<0, λ>√(−c) and all these curvatures are ≥√(−c). Must every boundary point admit an internally tangent radius-R ball contained in D, where the curvature-c space-form sphere of radius R has principal curvature λ?

**Context.** Origin for question 1833: Author’s selected questions in §6. Setup for question 1833: Manifolds are smooth and complete. Busemann functions use b_γ(y)=lim_{t→∞}(t−d(y,γ(t))).

**Source.** Kostiantyn Drach. *The Blaschke Rolling Theorem in Riemannian Manifolds of Bounded Curvature*. 2024. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s12220-026-02436-7) Location: §6, Question 3, final conjecture.

<a id="q1918"></a>

## Q1918. Generic scalar-curvature kernel

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1.15) · **Collection** 20

Does every neighborhood of ω within [ω] contain ω′ with ker L_ω′ consisting only of constants?

**Context.** Origin for question 1918: Thesis Question 4.1.15; antecedent discussion in Brown’s 2024 paper, §4. Setup for question 1918: M is compact Kähler. Write L_ωφ=(d/dt)|₀ S(ω+ti∂∂̄φ), the scalar-curvature linearization within its Kähler class.

**Source.** Garrett Michael Brown. *Scalar Curvature Under Blowup and Positive Scalar Curvature Kähler Surfaces*. 2026. Advisor(s): Song Sun and Richard Bamler. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt5zp3d8hc/qt5zp3d8hc.pdf) Location: Question 4.1.15, p. 38.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

<a id="q2030"></a>

## Q2030. Bounded torus-cotangent systolic ratio

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.7) · **Collection** 21

Is sup{ρ(X):X is dynamically convex} finite?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2030, 2031: Question 1.7; Conjecture 1.9. Setup for questions 2030, 2031: X⊂T\*T² is a smooth compact fiberwise star-shaped domain, T²=R²/Z², α=λ\_can|∂X. Dynamically convex means every contractible Reeb orbit has lower Conley–Zehnder index≥3, using a spanning disk. Set ρ(X)=sys(α)²/∫∂Xα∧dα, where sys is the least closed-Reeb action. A capacity is monotone under symplectic embeddings, scales linearly with ω, and is normalized by c(B⁴(r))=c(B²(r)×R²)=πr².

**Source.** Jun Zhang, Antong Zhu. *Geometry and dynamics on Liouville domains in T\*T²*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29253) Location: Question 1.7, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2030, 2031, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution located; product-domain results cover proper subclasses.

## Q2037. Quadrics for local uniform hypersurfaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 21

For n≥3, must every connected component of supp μ for a locally n-uniform μ on R^{n+1} lie in the zero set of a nonzero polynomial of degree at most two?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2037, 2038: Quadric conjecture: Kowalski–Preiss 1987. Analyticity: authors’ local extension of Kirchheim–Preiss. Setup for questions 2037, 2038: A Radon measure μ on R^{n+1} is locally k-uniform if μ(B(x,r))=ω\_k r^k for x∈supp μ and 0&lt;r≤1; ω\_k is unit-ball volume. It is locally uniformly distributed if 0<μ(B(x,r))=μ(B(y,r))<∞ for all support points x,y and 0&lt;r≤1.

**Source.** Max Engelstein; Ignasi Guillén-Mola. *Unique continuation for locally uniformly distributed measures*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13869) Location: Conjecture 1, Introduction, p. 2

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2037, 2038, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no matching resolution.

## Q2128. Inverse Levi property

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.10) · **Collection** 22

If n≥3 and K⊂Cⁿ is closed with smooth boundary and Cⁿ∖K is Oka, must ∂K be pseudoconvex?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2128, 2129, 2130, 2131: §1; Problems 3.5,3.10(a,b). Setup for questions 2128, 2129, 2130, 2131: Y is Oka if, for every n, holomorphic maps near compact convex K⊂Cⁿ are uniformly approximable on K by entire maps Cⁿ→Y. Pseudoconvex boundaries use the enclosed-set orientation. Fano means smooth projective with ample anticanonical bundle.

**Source.** Franc Forstnerič. *From Stein manifolds to Oka manifolds: the h-principle in complex analysis*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21197v1) Location: Problem 3.10(a), p. 10.

## Q2132. Approaching Kobayashi geodesics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 22

For every d≥1 and bounded convex domain D⊂Cᵈ whose Kobayashi metric k_D is Gromov hyperbolic, does (D,k_D) have approaching geodesics?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2132, 2133: Questions 2,5; approaching property introduced by Arosio–Fiacchi–Gontard–Guerini. Setup for questions 2132, 2133: All rays and almost-geodesics have domain [0,∞). Approaching geodesics means: for unit-speed rays γ,η with sup_t d(γ(t),η(t))<∞, some a∈R satisfies d(γ(t),η(t+a))→0. An almost-geodesic σ satisfies: for every ε>0, some T_ε ensures, for all s,t≥T_ε, |t−s|−ε≤d(σ(t),σ(s))≤|t−s|.

**Source.** Leandro Arosio; Matteo Fiacchi. *On the approaching geodesics property*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05876v1) Location: Question 2, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2132, 2133, checked 6 October 2026: no resolutions located; August exponential results require strong convexity or CAT(−1).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40574-024-00442-7) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.09848) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17259v3) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03986v2)

## Q2134. Lens spaces as maximum-volume isospectral pairs

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3) · **Collection** 22

For every odd d≥5, must each maximum-volume pair of nonisometric isospectral spherical space forms consist of lens spaces?

**Context.** Origin for question 2134: Lauret–Linowitz 2024, Question 4.7; recalled after Question 1.3. Setup for question 2134: Space forms are S^d/Γ with Γ≤O(d+1) finite and free, carrying curvature 1. Isospectrality concerns the function Laplacian with multiplicities. Lens spaces have cyclic Γ.

**Source.** Alfredo Álzaga; Emilio A. Lauret. *Isospectral spherical space forms and orbifolds of highest volume*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.33044/revuma.4943) Location: After Question 1.3, p. 73.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2134, checked 6 October 2026: fresh final-journal read retains the conjecture; September nonstrong-isospectral examples do not optimize volume. No resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B7: submitted unresolved): Experiment gives no all-dimensional maximum-volume result: scope-nonmatch. Remaining/limits: scope-nonmatch Intake: No maximum-volume comparison provided; exact target unchanged. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Counterexample (to a stronger or nearby claim)** (B7-R20: submitted disproved): The proposed implication from scalar-isospectral lens spaces to additively homometric signed weight sets fails for L(11;1,2,3), L(11;1,2,4). Remaining/limits: The low-dimensional, nonisomorphic-group, and all-dimensional maximum-volume questions remain unchanged. Refutes this stronger signed-weight transfer only, not any retained catalog statement. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://www.inmabb.criba.edu.ar/revuma/pdf/v69n1/v69n1a06.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02213v2) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01920)

## Q2145. RBCP without smooth renorming in ZFC

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.1) · **Collection** 22

Does ZFC give a separable scattered K such that C(K) has RBCP but has no equivalent Fréchet-smooth norm?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2145, 2146: Authors’ new questions. Setup for questions 2145, 2146: K is compact Hausdorff. A real Banach space has RBCP if, for every equivalent norm, its unit sphere is covered by countably many open balls none containing zero. Scattered means every nonempty subset has an isolated point.

**Source.** Rui Liu; Jie Shen; Richard J. Smith. *The renorming-stable ball covering property and C(K)-spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05846) Location: Problem 4.1, pp. 10–11

## Q2233. Standard-generator Rips contractibility

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

**Source.** Seth Hulbert; Matthew C. B. Zaremsky. *Word length, Morse theory, and Vietoris–Rips complexes*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25614) Location: Introduction after Theorem B, p. 2; Zaremsky §4.5.

<a id="q2240"></a>

## Q2240. Exponent continuity at maximal entropy in C1

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.9) · **Collection** 23

If f is SPR, must some χ′>0 ensure that every ergodic ν\_k converging weakly to μ with h_{ν\_k}(f)→h_top(f) has |λ\_i(f,x)|>χ′ for μ-almost every x and λ\_i(f,ν\_k)→λ\_i(f,μ), i=1,2?

**Context.** Origin for question 2240: Buzzi–Crovisier–Sarig exponent-continuity characterization; C¹ extension asked here. Setup for question 2240: Let f be a C¹ diffeomorphism of a compact Riemannian surface M, h_top(f)>0. Λ(χ,ε,C) consists of x admitting T_xM=E⊕F whose transported orbit splitting satisfies max{||Dfⁿ|DfᵏE||,||Df⁻ⁿ|DfᵏF||}≤Ce^{ε|k|−χn} for k∈Z,n≥0. SPR means ∃χ>0 ∀0<ε<χ ∃h₀&lt;h_top(f),C,m>0: every ergodic invariant probability ν with h_ν(f)>h₀ has ν(Λ)≥m. Write λ\_i(f,μ)=∫λ\_i(f,x)dμ for its Lyapunov exponents.

**Source.** Jérôme Buzzi. *Chaos on surfaces and beyond: a new notion of dynamical hyperbolicity*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30939) Location: Question 3.9;Theorem 3.7, pp. 7–8

**Literature check.** Status for question 2240, checked 6 October 2026: bounded-negative searches found no matching resolution.

## Q2423. Volume control of a shortest closed geodesic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For each n≥2, is there C_n<∞ such that every closed connected Riemannian n-manifold M has a nonconstant closed geodesic of length at most C_n·Vol(M)^(1/n)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2423, 2424: Gromov (1983), Introduction p. 1; Guth (2010), §7, reiterated §1.4 p. 7. Setup for questions 2423, 2424: For the waist question, waist₁(M)=inf_σ sup_t length(σ⁻¹(t)); σ:M→R² is continuous, its fibers are Lipschitz integral 1-cycles, and t↦σ⁻¹(t) is continuous in the cycle-space topology.

**Source.** Omar Alshawa; Herng Yi Cheng. *Riemannian 3-spheres that are hard to sweep out by short curves*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10904) Location: Introduction p. 1; 2026 thesis abstract.

## Q2431. Lawson Klein bottle minimizes Willmore energy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Among all C² immersions of a Klein bottle into S³, is the least possible Willmore energy attained by τ₁,₂?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2431, 2432: Kusner (1989), Conjecture 5; Bernstein–Ketover, Question 8.4. Setup for questions 2431, 2432: Use the unit round S³. C is a great circle. For closed immersed surfaces W=∫(1+|H|²/4)dA, with H the trace mean-curvature vector. τ₁,₂ is Lawson’s immersed minimal Klein bottle.

**Source.** Jacob Bernstein; Daniel Ketover. *On the Willmore energy of Möbius bands*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26745) Location: Conjecture 5, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2431, 2432, checked 7 October 2026: September 22 v1 retains both targets; targeted searches through 7 October 2026 found no later resolution.

## Q2530. Curvature characterization of extreme constant-width bodies

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Is K extreme if and only if r_min(u)=0 or r_max(u)=1 for almost every u∈S², with respect to spherical area?

**Context.** Origin for question 2530: Hynd’s conjectured three-dimensional analogue of Kallay’s planar characterization. Setup for question 2530: Let K⊂R³ be a compact convex body of constant width one, and h(u)=max_(x∈K)⟨x,u⟩ on S². Let r_min(u)≤r_max(u) be the almost-everywhere eigenvalues of ∇²\_(S²)h+hI. Call K extreme if every Minkowski decomposition K=(1−t)K₀+tK₁, 0&lt;t<1, into width-one bodies has K₀,K₁ translates of each other.

**Source.** Ryan Hynd. *On extreme constant width bodies in R³. Discrete & Computational Geometry 76*. 2026. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00454-025-00779-6) Location: Unnumbered Conjecture, §1; curvature formulation and discussion in §6.

## Q2532. Measure-zero three-periodic outer symplectic billiards

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Must {x∈R^(2n)∖K:T³(x)=x} have 2n-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero?

**Context.** Origin for question 2532: Tabachnikov’s explicitly posed three-periodic higher-dimensional outer-billiard problem. Setup for question 2532: Let K⊂R^(2n) be a smooth strictly convex compact body with nonempty interior, n≥2, and J the standard complex structure. Its outer symplectic billiard map T on R^(2n)∖K is defined by T(x)=2q−x, where q∈∂K is the unique point with x=q+tJν(q), t>0, and ν(q) the outward unit normal.

**Source.** Serge Tabachnikov. *contribution to Open problems in billiards and quantitative symplectic geometry*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.12896v1) Location: Tabachnikov contribution, “Ivrii’s conjecture for outer symplectic billiards”, final Problem.

## Q2718. Smooth inverse for the reduced tangent pseudobundle

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Is the fiberwise-bijective map (TΣ(Z)/V)/G→TΣ(Q) induced by the quotient projection an isomorphism of differentiable pseudobundles, with differentiable inverse?

**Context.** Origin for question 2718: Ross’s conjecture; not a preexisting named problem. Setup for question 2718: Let a connected Lie group G act properly and Hamiltonianly on (M,ω), with moment map J:M→𝔤\*. Retain §10.2’s equivariant prequantum line bundle generated over Z=J⁻¹(0) by invariant sections. Put Q=Z/G. TΣ(Z), TΣ(Q) are the differentiable stratified tangent pseudobundles for the orbit-type and induced quotient stratifications, and V⊂TΣ(Z) consists of orbit-tangent vectors.

**Source.** Ethan Ross. *Stratified Pseudobundles and Quantization*. 2025. Advisor(s): Lisa Jeffrey. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.03544) Location: §10.2, equation (10.5), p. 244.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2718, checked 7 October 2026: January 2026 thesis version states it; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03544) · [2](https://ethanross.ca/academic/)

## Q2720. Bounded spectral diameter in every cotangent disk bundle

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

For every N, is sup{dγ(L,L′):L,L′∈Lag_ex(D\*N)} finite?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2720: Giovanni Ambrosioni. On some quantitative aspects of categorical Lagrangian topology. PhD, ETH Zürich, 2026; supervisor Paul Biran. Origin for question 2720: Viterbo’s spectral boundedness conjecture, restated by the authors. Setup for question 2720: For a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold N, let D\*N be its unit cotangent disk bundle and Lag_ex its closed embedded λ-exact Lagrangians lying in the interior. Use Z₂ Floer coefficients. The spectral distance dγ is the limsup difference between the largest and smallest births of infinite bars in HF(L,L′;H), as ||H||C⁰→0, as in Remark 3.7.1.

**Source.** Giovanni Ambrosioni, Paul Biran, Octav Cornea. *Approximability for Lagrangian submanifolds*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.12506) Location: Introduction p. 4, before Corollary 1.1.3; metric §3.7, Remark 3.7.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2720, checked 7 October 2026: January 2026 paper explicitly leaves general boundedness open; no later general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12506) · [2](https://giovanniambrosioni.github.io/) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.13700) · [4](https://math.ethz.ch/news-and-events/news/d-math-news/2026/03/doctoral-exam-of-giovanni-ambrosioni.html)


<a id="q2725"></a>

## Q2725. Codimension-two volumes rigidify generic manifolds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 28

For every d≥3 and every finite connected simplicial (d−1)-manifold without boundary, is every generic realization of its (d−2)-faces in Rᵈ volume-rigid?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2725, 2726: Conjecture and closing question of the authors. Setup for questions 2725, 2726: Realizations place the vertices in Rᵈ. Generic means all vertex coordinates are algebraically independent over Q. Volume-rigid means every continuous vertex motion preserving each indicated face’s Euclidean volume is congruent to the initial realization. Infinitesimal volume-rigidity means every velocity assignment with zero first derivative of all squared indicated volumes is an infinitesimal Euclidean isometry.

**Source.** James Cruickshank, Bill Jackson, Shin-ichi Tanigawa. *Volume Rigidity of Simplicial Manifolds*. 2026. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00493-026-00218-x) Location: Conjecture 2; preprint p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2725, 2726, checked 7 October 2026: June 2026 journal-matching revision retains both; the generic codimension-two case is proved for d≤6. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01647v2)

## Q2729. Nonextendable higher-order actions with two blowups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

For every even m≥4, does there exist an irrational ruled symplectic 4-manifold with base genus at least two and b₂=4 carrying an effective homologically trivial, nonextendable symplectic Z/m-action?

**Context.** Origin for question 2729: Authors’ question after their circle-extension theorem. Setup for question 2729: An irrational ruled symplectic 4-manifold is a symplectic blowup of an S²-bundle over a closed orientable positive-genus surface. Homologically trivial means identity on integral homology. “Nonextendable” means the given cyclic action is not the restriction of a Hamiltonian S¹-action for any symplectic form on the same smooth manifold.

**Source.** Nicholas Lindsay, Weiyi Zhang. *Periodic symplectic and Hamiltonian diffeomorphisms on irrational ruled surfaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2026.10265) Location: §5, pp. 25–26, between Theorem 5.1 and Corollary 5.2.

## Q2919. Cubic planar surfaces beyond doubles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.7) · **Collection** 30

Does some cubic planar Legendrian fail to be Legendrian isotopic to Λ(L₁,L₂) for every n and every pair of fillings of λ(2,n)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2919, 2920: Agniva Roy. Constructions and Invariants of High-Dimensional Legendrian Submanifolds. PhD, Georgia Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisors: John Etnyre. Origin for questions 2919, 2920: Hughes–Roy, following Roy’s doctoral work. Setup for questions 2919, 2920: Links lie in standard contact R³; λ(2,n) is the maximal-tb (2,n) torus link. Λ(L₁,L₂) is the Legendrian double in standard R⁵ of embedded exact Lagrangian fillings. Cubic planar Legendrians arise from planar cubic graphs by Treumann–Zaslow’s construction. For braid-positive λ whose microlocal-rank-one sheaf moduli has a cluster structure, consider exact fillings equipped with L-compressing systems realizing cluster seeds. Their mutation distance dμ is the minimum number of Lagrangian disk surgeries connecting them up to Hamiltonian isotopy.

**Source.** James Hughes, Agniva Roy. *Legendrian doubles, twist spuns, and clusters*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.17901v1) Location: Question 1.7, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2919, 2920, checked 7 October 2026: The current v1 retains both; no later resolution found.

## Q2922. Hyperbolic convex-core metric rigidity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.3) · **Collection** 30

Let M be the interior of a compact 3-manifold with nonempty boundary, every component having genus at least two, admitting a convex-cocompact hyperbolic structure. Does the induced hyperbolic metric on the entire convex-core boundary uniquely determine the structure?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2922, 2923: Thurston’s induced-metric question and its anti-de Sitter analogue due to Mess. Setup for questions 2922, 2923: All structures and boundary data are marked; uniqueness means up to marking-preserving isometry. A convex core is the smallest nonempty geodesically convex subset.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 30

For a closed oriented surface S of genus at least two, is a globally hyperbolic maximal Cauchy-compact anti-de Sitter 3-spacetime on S×R uniquely determined by the two induced hyperbolic metrics on its convex-core boundary?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2922, 2923: Thurston’s induced-metric question and its anti-de Sitter analogue due to Mess. Setup for questions 2922, 2923: All structures and boundary data are marked; uniqueness means up to marking-preserving isometry. A convex core is the smallest nonempty geodesically convex subset.

**Source.** Jean-Marc Schlenker. *Volume functions and boundary data of 3-dimensional hyperbolic manifolds, ICM 2026 survey*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05627v1) Location: Question 5.1, first bullet, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2922, 2923, checked 7 October 2026: The 4 October 2026 preprint proves AdS smooth-boundary and bending rigidity; convex-core induced-metric rigidity remains open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05627) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05506v1)


<a id="q2928"></a>

## Q2928. Global CMC foliation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Does every almost-Fuchsian manifold admit a foliation by closed incompressible constant-mean-curvature surfaces?

**Context.** Origin for question 2928: Thurston’s CMC question, repeated in the thesis. Setup for question 2928: Let S be a closed oriented surface of genus at least two. An almost-Fuchsian hyperbolic 3-manifold is quasifuchsian, homeomorphic to S×R, and contains a closed incompressible minimal surface with principal curvatures in (−1,1).

**Source.** Diptaishik Choudhury. *Measured foliations at infinity and constant mean curvature surfaces in quasi-Fuchsian manifolds close to the Fuchsian locus*. 2023. Advisor(s): Jean-Marc Schlenker, Greg McShane and Andrea Seppi. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.13682v1) Location: §1.2, pp. 6–7; thesis abstract.

<a id="q2936"></a>

## Q2936. Foliations at infinity always fill

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 30

Does (F_+(M),F_−(M)) fill S for every such M?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2936, 2937: see question 2928. Origin for question 2936: Schlenker’s filling question, restated by Choudhury–Marković after the doctoral work. Setup for questions 2936, 2937: Fix a closed oriented surface S of genus at least two. For a marked non-Fuchsian quasifuchsian M≅S×R with conformal ends X,Y, set q_+=β\_X(Y), q_−=β\_Y(X), using the Bers embeddings. Let F_±(M) be the horizontal measured foliations of q_±. A pair fills S if i(F_+,μ)+i(F_−,μ)>0 for every nonzero measured lamination μ.

**Source.** Diptaishik Choudhury, Vladimir Marković. *Measured foliations at infinity of quasi-Fuchsian manifolds*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.04111v1) Location: Question 1.6, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2936, 2937, checked 7 October 2026: The paper proves results near the Fuchsian locus. Later searches found no general resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04111) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05627) · [3](https://publications.uni.lu/handle/10993/54995) · [4](https://orbilu.uni.lu/bitstream/10993/54995/1/DiptaishikChoudhurythesisfinalversion%20%281%29.pdf) · [5](https://math.uni.lu/schlenker/group.html) · [6](https://andreaseppi.github.io/Supervision.html)

<a id="q3026"></a>

## Q3026. Dehn spectra of free solvable groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.4) · **Collection** 31

Determine f_{F_r/F_r^(d)} up to ∼ for finitely generated free solvable groups (r,d≥2).

**Context.** Origin for questions 3026, 3027: Osin–Rybak paper-origin questions. Setup for questions 3026, 3027: For finite generators X, let S_m be all null words of length≤m. Put f_G(k,m,n)=max_{|w|≤n,w∈⟨⟨S_k⟩⟩}Area_{S_m}(w), m≥k≥1, where Area counts conjugates of relators. Write f≼g if some integer C≥1 gives f(k,Cm,n)≤Cg(Ck,m,Cn)+Cn/m+C whenever m≥Ck; f∼g means both inequalities. F_r^(d) is the dth derived subgroup. Hyperbolic approximation means G=⟨X|∪R_i⟩ with nested R_i and hyperbolic G_i=⟨X|R_i⟩. It is well-approximated if some C∈N ensures, for every i, that all G-null words of length≤i lie in ⟨⟨R_i⟩⟩ and Cay(G_i,X) is Ci-hyperbolic.

**Source.** Denis Osin and Ekaterina Rybak. *Isoperimetric inequalities in finitely generated groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s00209-026-03992-y.pdf) Location: Problem 6.4, p. 42.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3026, 3027, checked 8 October 2026: March 2026 journal retains both problems; latest arXiv version is v4.

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00209-026-03992-y) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.12518) · [3](https://sites.google.com/view/ekaterinarybak/home)

Hyperbolic approximation means G=⟨X|∪R_i⟩ with nested R_i and hyperbolic G_i=⟨X|R_i⟩. ## Q3031. Asymptotic slopes of toric capacities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

For every smooth compact star-shaped toric domain X⊂C², does lim_{k→∞}c_k^GH(X)/k exist?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3031, 3032: Shah Faisal. Extremal Lagrangian tori in toric domains. PhD, Humboldt University of Berlin, 2024. Advisors: Klaus Mohnke. Origin for questions 3031, 3032: The half-ball target is stated here; its capacity comes from Cieliebak–Mohnke. Slope target: Irie’s 2023 proposal. Setup for questions 3031, 3032: Use standard symplectic Cⁿ and B^{2n}(1)={πΣ|z_i|²≤1}. For a closed embedded Lagrangian L⊂X, put A_min(L)=inf{∫\_Aω>0:A∈π₂(X,L)}. Set c_all(X)=sup_L A_min(L), allowing every closed Lagrangian topology, with inf∅=∞. A smooth compact star-shaped toric domain is invariant under coordinatewise rotations and has radial Liouville field outward transverse to its boundary. c_k^GH denotes the kth Gutt–Hutchings capacity.

**Source.** Shah Faisal and Yin Li. *Lagrangian capacity and chain level string topology*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.20051v2) Location: Remark 11, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3031, 3032, checked 8 October 2026: Both remain conjectural in the July 16 revision; the aspherical-capacity theorem does not resolve the all-Lagrangian value.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20051) · [2](https://www.shahfaisal.net/home) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.08720) · [4](https://opus.bibliothek.uni-augsburg.de/opus4/files/54954/54954.pdf) · [5](https://edoc.hu-berlin.de/bitstreams/c5a7cb95-49b3-4ef7-bd63-68ec6d75ac4a/download)

## Q3118. Automorphisms of geodesic currents

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.2) · **Collection** 32

Is Aut(C(S))≅Mod±(S) for every such S?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3118: Meenakshy Jyothis. PhD, 2025, Binghamton University. Advisors: Eugenia Sapir. Origin for question 3118: Paper-origin question, preceding the dissertation. Setup for question 3118: For a closed orientable surface S of genus≥2, C(S) is the weak-\* space of π₁(S)-invariant Radon measures on unoriented geodesics of its universal cover. Aut(C(S)) comprises homeomorphisms preserving Bonahon’s intersection form. Mod±(S) is the extended mapping class group.

**Source.** Meenakshy Jyothis. *Towards Ivanov’s meta-conjecture for geodesic currents*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.14532v3) Location: Question 6.2, p. 19; definitions pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3118, checked 8 October 2026: The February 2025 revision retains the question; the 2026 filling-current rigidity theorem concerns a different automorphism structure.

## Q3119. Quasi-isometry distinguishes genera for filling currents

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 10.2) · **Collection** 32

For g≠g′, are PC_fill(S_g) and PC_fill(S_g′) non-quasi-isometric with d_sym, and likewise with d?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3119: see question 3118. Origin for question 3119: Joint paper-origin question. Setup for question 3119: S_g is closed orientable of genus g≥2. PC_fill(S_g) is the projectivization of filling geodesic currents: i(μ,ν)>0 for every nonzero current ν. Write ℓ\_μ(γ)=i(μ,γ), h(μ)=lim_{R→∞}R⁻¹log#{[γ]≠1:ℓ\_μ(γ)≤R}, d([μ],[ν])=log sup_[γ] h(ν)ℓ\_ν(γ)/(h(μ)ℓ\_μ(γ)), and d_sym(μ,ν)=d(μ,ν)+d(ν,μ).

**Source.** Meenakshy Jyothis and Dídac Martínez-Granado. *Horoboundary and rigidity of filling geodesic currents*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.02059v1) Location: Question 10.2, p. 49; equations (1), (3).

**Literature check.** Status for question 3119, checked 8 October 2026: January source retains the target. The October rank paper distinguishes filling currents from Teichmüller space, not different genera.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03359v1)


<a id="q3120"></a>

## Q3120. Regularity of Manhattan curves for filling currents

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.20) · **Collection** 32

Must θ\_{αβ}:R→R be C²? Must it be real analytic?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3120: see question 3118. Origin for question 3120: Joint paper-origin question. Setup for question 3120: Let α,β be filling geodesic currents on a closed orientable surface S of genus≥2. Put ℓ\_α(γ)=i(α,γ), and define θ\_{αβ}(t) as the convergence abscissa in s of Σ\_[γ]≠1 exp(−sℓ\_α(γ)−tℓ\_β(γ)); its graph is the Manhattan curve.

**Source.** Meenakshy Jyothis and Dídac Martínez-Granado. *Geodesic currents of coarse negative curvature*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.14469v1) Location: Question 7.20, p. 37; §7.3.

## Q3124. Almost planarity and planar quasi-isometry

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 32

Is every connected locally finite quasi-transitive almost-planar graph quasi-isometric to a planar graph?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3124, 3125: Joseph P. MacManus. Splittings, tiles, and planarity: a trio of problems in geometric group theory. PhD, 2025, University of Oxford. Advisors: Panos Papazoglou. Origin for questions 3124, 3125: Planarity: Georgakopoulos–Papasoglu; logarithmic-separator question is posed here, following Hume–Mackay. Setup for questions 3124, 3125: Graphs carry the path metric. Quasi-transitive means finitely many vertex orbits under automorphisms. A graph is almost planar if it has a plane drawing where each edge crosses at most k other edges, for some finite k. Define sep_Γ(r)=sup_{F⊂Γ,|V(F)|≤r}cut(F), where cut(F) is the fewest vertices whose deletion leaves components of at most |V(F)|/2 vertices.

**Source.** John M. Mackay, Joseph P. MacManus and Davide Spriano. *Almost planar finitely presented groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.03040v1) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3124, 3125, checked 8 October 2026: May paper proves the planarity target for coarsely simply connected graphs. July hyperbolic planar separator bounds do not settle the converses.

**Further links.** [1](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3A11d13945-2ddb-4159-8d79-bb29f9f36569)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1.6) · **Collection** 32

For k≥1, is c_k(X×\_pY)=min_{i+j=k, i,j≥0}(c_i(X)^{p/(p−2)}+c_j(Y)^{p/(p−2)})^{(p−2)/p} when p>2, and max_{i+j=k+1, i,j≥1}(c_i(X)^{p/(p−2)}+c_j(Y)^{p/(p−2)})^{(p−2)/p} when 1≤p<2? At p=2, is it the kth term of the merged capacity sequences, with multiplicity?

All indices below are integers.

[primary source](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1CyNW_Dfh9Nqb8zQbwPFmLHJeG1QeZsju/view) Location: Conjecture 3.1.6 and Remark 3.1.7, pp. 51–52; survey Conjecture 4.17.
