<a id="q3128"></a>

## Q3128. Symmetric Viterbo inequality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 32

For every centrally symmetric K, must c(K)^n≤n!vol_{2n}(K) hold for every normalized symplectic capacity c?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3128: see question 3126. Origin for question 3128: Haim-Kislev–Ostrover’s symmetric Viterbo question. Setup for question 3128: K⊂(R^{2n},ω₀), n≥2, is a compact convex body. A symplectic capacity is normalized by c(B^{2n}(r))=c(B²(r)×R^{2n−2})=πr².

**Source.** Pazit Haim-Kislev and Yaron Ostrover. *A counterexample to Viterbo’s conjecture*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.16513v3) Location: Question 1, p. 6; normalization pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3128, checked 8 October 2026: The current source and 2026 survey retain this question; September results concern specified rotated ℓ\_p-ellipsoids.

**Further links.** [1](https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2026/203-2/p05) · [2](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1jwjSiABmg1dZloyC11_dfKxbHzfrlXGd/view) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37794v1)


<a id="q3129"></a>

## Q3129. Distance from convex to monotone toric domains

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

For every n≥2, determine sup_{K∈C_n}inf_{T∈M_n}d_c(K,T).

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3129, 3130: see question 3126. Origin for questions 3129, 3130: New quantitative questions in the author’s survey. Setup for questions 3129, 3130: For compact domains in standard R^{2n}, define d_c(X,Y)=inf{log λ:λ≥1,(1/λ)X symplectically embeds into Y and Y into λX}. A toric domain is μ⁻¹(Ω), μ(z)=(π|z₁|²,…,π|z_n|²); it is monotone when the outward normals of its moment boundary in the positive orthant have nonnegative coordinates. Let C_n be convex bodies and M_n monotone toric domains.

**Source.** Pazit Haim-Kislev. *On symplectic measurements of convex domains*. 2025. [primary source](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1jwjSiABmg1dZloyC11_dfKxbHzfrlXGd/view) Location: §5.2, “Distance of convex domains from being toric”, pp. 47–48.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3129, 3130, checked 8 October 2026: The current author manuscript retains both questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.pazithaimkislev.com/)

## Q3133. Maximal systolic index of convex bodies

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 32

For each n≥2, determine sup_K I(K). Is this supremum n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3133, 3134: Paper-origin questions arising from the author’s doctoral work. Setup for questions 3133, 3134: Let K⊂R^{2n} be a compact convex body with nonempty interior. Write c_k for its Gutt–Hutchings capacities with F₂ coefficients, I(K)=max{k:c_k(K)=c₁(K)}, and ρ(K)=c₁(K)^n/(n!vol(K)). Call K generalized Zoll when I(K)≥n. Use Hausdorff distance on convex bodies.

**Source.** Stefan Matijević. *Systolic S¹-index and characterization of non-smooth Zoll convex bodies*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.13856v3) Location: Question 1, p. 6; Theorems 1.2 and 1.7.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3133, 3134, checked 8 October 2026: The November 2025 revision retains these questions. Its solved earlier Question 1 concerned a different equivariant-map problem; later wild-ellipsoid constructions do not resolve these targets.

**Further links.** [1](https://exact-sciences.tau.ac.il/math/postdocs)

## Q3221. Monotonicity of p-Kähler existence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 33

For integers 1≤p≤q≤n−1, must every compact complex n-manifold admitting a p-Kähler structure admit a q-Kähler structure?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3221: Asia Mainenti. p-Kähler and balanced structures on complex manifolds. PhD, 2025, University of Turin. Advisors: Anna Maria Fino. Origin for question 3221: Alessandrini–Bassanelli’s older conjecture, restated in this post-thesis paper; not thesis-origin. Setup for question 3221: A p-Kähler structure on a complex n-manifold is a smooth real d-closed (p,p)-form restricting to a positive volume form on every complex p-plane in each tangent space.

**Source.** Ettore Lo Giudice and Asia Mainenti. *p-Kähler structures on nilmanifolds and holomorphically parallelizable manifolds*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29506v1) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 1; compact-manifold context §4.

## Q3222. Coexistence of special Hermitian metrics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

For n>2, does admitting metrics of any two of these three types force the manifold to admit a Kähler metric? The two metrics need not coincide.

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3222: see question 3221. Origin for question 3222: Ornea–Verbitsky’s older extension of the Fino–Vezzoni conjecture, restated after Mainenti’s thesis. Setup for question 3222: With d^c=JdJ⁻¹ on a compact complex n-manifold, a Hermitian fundamental form ω is balanced if d(ω^(n−1))=0, pluriclosed if dd^cω=0, and locally conformally Kähler if dω=θ∧ω for a closed real one-form θ.

**Source.** Asia Mainenti and Andrei Moroianu. *Special structures on almost abelian solvmanifolds*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.09583v2) Location: Introduction, p. 3, paragraph citing [26].

**Literature check.** Status for question 3222, checked 8 October 2026: The June 2026 revision retains the general conjecture; August–September parallel-torsion results concern additional hypotheses.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.polito.it/didattica/dottorato-di-ricerca-e-scuola-di-specializzazione/alumni?p=091325)

## Q3223. Weighted polygonal perimeter deviations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 33

Is (p̄\_n) convex for every K,C and conjugate p,q∈[1,∞]?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3223, 3224, 3225: Bushra Basit. Geometric Extremum Problems. PhD, 2025, Budapest University of Technology and Economics. Advisors: Zsolt Lángi. Origin for questions 3223, 3224, 3225: Paper-origin problems, preceding the thesis. Setup for questions 3223, 3224, 3225: Let C be an origin-symmetric plane convex body, ℓ\_C its normed arclength, and 1/p+1/q=1. For convex bodies K,L define A=p|K∖L|+q|L∖K| and P=p[ℓ\_C(∂K∖int L)−ℓ\_C(∂L∩K)]+q[ℓ\_C(∂L∖int K)−ℓ\_C(∂K∩L)]. Use ∞·0=0 and n≥3. Let â\_n,p̂\_n be the infima of A,P over intersections of n translates of C; p̄\_n is the infimum of P over convex n-gons. C-convex means an intersection of translates of C.

**Source.** Bushra Basit and Zsolt Lángi. *Dowker-type theorems for disk-polygons in normed planes*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.disc.2024.114019) Location: §5 Problem 1, final article p. 14.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3223, 3224, 3225, checked 8 October 2026: The author-uploaded final article retains Problems 1–3 unchanged.

## Q3227. Unstable Laplace multiplicity seven

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.7) · **Collection** 33

Does some such (M,g) have an unstable Laplace eigenvalue of multiplicity exactly seven?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3227, 3228: Willi Kepplinger. Parametric spectral theory with an eye towards applications to contact geometry. PhD, 2025, University of Vienna. Advisors: Vera Vértesi. Origin for questions 3227, 3228: Joint paper-origin questions. Setup for questions 3227, 3228: Let G(M) be the C∞ space of smooth Riemannian metrics on a closed connected smooth manifold M of dimension≥2. A Laplace eigenvalue λ is unstable if some real L²-orthogonal eigenbasis u₁,…,u_m and nonzero ε∈{−1,0,1}^m satisfy Σ ε\_ju_j²=0 and Σ ε\_jdu_j⊗du_j=0.

**Source.** Josef Greilhuber and Willi Kepplinger. *On Arnold’s Transversality Conjecture for the Laplace–Beltrami Operator*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.16939v4) Location: Question 4.7, p. 31; Theorem 1.1, p. 3; Proposition 2.3 proof p. 12 (nonzero relation).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3227, 3228, checked 8 October 2026: The May 2026 revision retains both questions; multiplicities≤6 are stable, while known unstable examples have multiplicity≥8.

**Further links.** [1](https://utheses.univie.ac.at/detail/76287)

<a id="q3230"></a>

## Q3230. Gömböc in every smooth normed three-space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 33

Does every such normed three-space admit a mono-monostatic convex body?

**Context.** Origin for question 3230: Joint paper-origin conjecture during doctoral study. Setup for question 3230: Let M⊂R³ be the unit ball of a norm, with C² boundary and strictly positive Gaussian curvature. For a convex body K of uniform density, c(K)=|K|⁻¹∫\_Kx dx. A K with C² boundary is mono-monostatic if the restriction of x↦‖x−c(K)‖\_M to ∂K has exactly two critical points, a nondegenerate minimum and a nondegenerate maximum.

**Source.** Zsolt Lángi and Shanshan Wang. *Equilibria in non-Euclidean geometries*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01159v2) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 3; centroid and equilibrium conventions §2, pp. 7–8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3230, checked 8 October 2026: The February 2026 paper proves existence for rotationally symmetric norms and leaves the unrestricted smooth strictly convex norm case open.

**Further links.** [1](https://shanshanwang-math.github.io/)

## Q3231. Minimum symmetric enclosure of a few-vertex polytope

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.1) · **Collection** 33

For d≥2 and d+3≤n≤2d−1, determine the minimum volume of an origin-symmetric convex polytope in R^d that contains a unit-volume convex polytope with n vertices.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3231, 3232: see question 3230. Origin for questions 3231, 3232: Joint paper-origin questions during doctoral study. Setup for questions 3231, 3232: Volume is Lebesgue measure. For an origin-symmetric plane convex body B, B°={y:⟨x,y⟩≤1 for all x∈B}; Q₆(B) is a maximum-area convex polygon contained in B with at most six vertices.

**Source.** Zsolt Lángi and Shanshan Wang. *Variants of a theorem of Macbeath in finite-dimensional normed spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/mtk.70078) Location: Problem 7.1, final article p. 16.

## Q3233. Honeycomb minimization for mean normed perimeter

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 33

In every normed plane, among normal tilings with unit-area cells and an existing mean normed perimeter, is the minimum attained by translates of one centrally symmetric unit-area hexagon?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3233: see question 3230. Origin for question 3233: Joint paper-origin questions. Setup for question 3233: For a convex plane tiling T, put T(R)={C∈T:C⊂RB²}, with B² the Euclidean unit disk, and ⟨f⟩₋=liminf_{R→∞}(Σ\_{C∈T(R)}f(C))/#T(R); define ⟨f⟩₊ with limsup. Normal means uniform positive lower inradius and upper circumradius bounds. Write s(C) for the number of sides; ir(C),cr(C) are Euclidean inradius and circumradius.

**Source.** Zsolt Lángi and Shanshan Wang. *The Honeycomb Conjecture in Normed Planes and an Alpha-Convex Variant of a Theorem of Dowker*. 2026. [primary source](https://publicatio.bibl.u-szeged.hu/38870/1/rnaf372.pdf) Location: Question 1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3233, checked 8 October 2026: The January 2026 final retains both questions; the squared-perimeter theorem is weaker.

**Further links.** [1](https://shanshanwang-math.github.io/)

## Q3329. Translated higher-order convex joins

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 9.3) · **Collection** 34

For every full-dimensional convex body K⊂R^n and every such t, does some x∈K satisfy |R_(p,t,K−x)|/|K|^p≤|R_(p,t,S_n)|/|S_n|^p?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3329: Jan Kotrbatý. Integral Geometry on the Octonionic Plane. PhD, 2020, Friedrich Schiller University Jena. Advisors: Thomas Wannerer. Origin for question 3329: Joint-paper conjecture extending an earlier Fáry–Rédei-type question. Setup for question 3329: For n≥2 and p≥1, let Δ\_p:R^n→(R^n)^p be the diagonal embedding and ι\_l the lth factor inclusion. For t_l≥0 with ∑\_(l=0)^p t_l=1, set R_(p,t,K)=conv(−t₀Δ\_pK ∪ ⋃\_(l=1)^p t_lι\_lK). Let S_n be an n-simplex with centroid zero; |·| denotes ambient-dimensional volume.

**Source.** Filip Fryš and Jan Kotrbatý. *Around higher-order Godbersen conjectures*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.08612v2) Location: Conjecture 9.3, p. 29.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3329, checked 8 October 2026: An October 2026 proof claim addresses mixed volumes, not this translated convex-hull bound.

<a id="q3342"></a>

## Q3342. Conformal minimality of full carpets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.2) · **Collection** 34

Must Cdim_H E=dim_H E?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3342, 3343: Final-paper Questions 8.2,8.4; not asserted dissertation-origin. Setup for questions 3342, 3343: Define Cdim_H X=inf_f dim_H f(X) and Cdim_B X=inf_f upper-dim_B f(X), over quasisymmetric homeomorphisms onto metric spaces; quasisymmetric means d(fx,fa)/d(fx,fb)≤η(d(x,a)/d(x,b)) for an increasing homeomorphism η:[0,∞)→[0,∞). A full Bedford–McMullen carpet E is the attractor of {(x,y)↦((x+i)/m,(y+j)/n):(i,j)∈𝒜}, with integers n>m≥2 and 𝒜⊂{0,…,m−1}×{0,…,n−1} meeting every column. Mandelbrot percolation F retains each of Mⁿ subcubes independently with probability p at every generation in [0,1]ⁿ; M≥2 and M^(−n)&lt;p<1. Condition on F≠∅. Put a_θ(X)=inf{s≥0:∃C ∀x∈X,0&lt;r≤R^(1/θ)&lt;R<1, N_r(B_X(x,R))≤C(R/r)^s}, q(X)=lim_{θ↑1}a_θ(X), where N_r counts radius-r covering balls. Define Ca_θ,Cq by the same quasisymmetric infima.

**Source.** Jonathan M. Fraser; Jeremy T. Tyson. *Sobolev and quasiconformal distortion of intermediate dimension with applications to conformal dimension*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms.70445) Location: Question 8.2; §2.4 and Example6.4

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3342, 3343, checked 8 October 2026: Final article retains these questions; no exact later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1112/jlms.70445) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10525v4) · [3](https://jonathan-fraser.github.io/homepage/papers.html)

## Q3347. Quasi-Assouad dimension with overlaps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 17.5.3) · **Collection** 34

Must q(E)=b(E) for every attractor E⊂ℝᵈ of a finite family of contracting similarities, without separation assumptions?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3347, 3348, 3349: Author-maintained list,8January2025, repeating2020book questions; no dissertation-origin claim. Setup for questions 3347, 3348, 3349: Write N_r(E) for the minimum number of radius-r balls covering E. Assouad dimension a(E) is inf{s:∃C ∀x∈E,0&lt;r&lt;R, N_r(E∩B(x,R))≤C(R/r)^s}. Define q(E)=lim_{θ↑1}inf{s:∃C ∀x∈E,0&lt;r≤R^(1/θ)&lt;R<1, N_r(E∩B(x,R))≤C(R/r)^s}. Write b(E)=limsup_{r↓0}log N_r(E)/(−log r), π\_V for orthogonal projection onto V∈G(k,d), and D_x(E)={|x−y|:y∈E}. Define ℓ(E)=sup{s≥0:∃c>0 ∀x∈E,0&lt;r&lt;R&lt;diam(E), N_r(E∩B(x,R))≥c(R/r)^s}.

**Source.** Jonathan M. Fraser. *Assouad dimension and fractal geometry: updates on open problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://jonathan-fraser.github.io/homepage/Chapter17.pdf) Location: Question 17.5.3, p. 3

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3347, 3348, 3349, checked 8 October 2026: The 2026 projection paper retains the self-similar problem; other retained targets had no exact resolution located.

## Q3410. Hierarchical hyperbolicity without CAT(0)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.4) · **Collection** 35

Does some unbranched Γ admit no proper cocompact isometric action on a CAT(0) space?

**Context.** Let Γ=F_n⋊\_φZ, n≥3, φ∈Out(F_n), with a word metric. Here “unbranched” is equivalent to hierarchically hyperbolic (Theorem A). Origin: Joint post-thesis paper, with Bongiovanni’s doctoral connection.

**Source.** Eliot Bongiovanni, Pritam Ghosh, Funda Gültepe and Mark Hagen. *Characterizing hierarchically hyperbolic free by cyclic groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.15738v3) Location: Question 1.4, p. 7.

<a id="q3467"></a>

## Q3467. Higher-dimensional nonarithmetic complex lattices

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

For which integers n≥4 does G contain a nonarithmetic lattice?

**Context.** Origin: Classical existence problem, explicitly restated in the thesis. Setup: Let G=PU(n,1), the holomorphic isometry group of complex hyperbolic n-space. A lattice Γ≤G is discrete with finite Haar covolume. It is arithmetic if, for a connected semisimple real matrix group H≤SL_m(R) defined over Q, a continuous surjection H→G with compact kernel sends H∩SL_m(Z) to a group commensurable with Γ. Here Γ₁,Γ₂ are commensurable if Γ₁∩gΓ₂g⁻¹ has finite index in both Γ₁ and gΓ₂g⁻¹ for some g∈G.

**Source.** Joseph Wells. *Hybrid subgroups of complex hyperbolic isometries*. Arizona State University, 2019. Advisor(s): Julien Paupert. [primary source](https://joseph-wells.com/papers/(thesis)hybrid_subgroups_of_complex_hyperbolic_isometries.pdf) Location: Introduction, p. 3; §2.2, pp. 7–8.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Later hybrid constructions inspected concern dimensions 2–3; no higher-dimensional construction or exclusion found through 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://joseph-wells.com/papers/(thesis)


<a id="q3526"></a>

## Q3526. Proper deformation in real dimension four

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Does every holomorphic Hamiltonian system on a real four-dimensional manifold admit a proper deformation of its associated triple (J,Re Ω,Re H)?

**Context.** Degree: Dr. rer. nat.; oral examination 4 November 2024; deposited 2025 Advisors: Kai Cieliebak. Source type: PhD dissertation, deposited 2025 Origin: Question following Theorem 2.3.16; earlier arXiv:2303.09480 establishes the higher-dimensional result. Setup: A pseudo-holomorphic Hamiltonian system on X is (J,ω,h), where ω is symplectic, J²=−1, ω(Ju,Jv)=−ω(u,v), h:X→ℝ is smooth, and ω(JX_h,·) is exact, with ι\_{X_h}ω=−dh. A proper deformation is a smooth path of such triples whose J_t is nonintegrable for every t≠0.

**Source.** Luiz Frederic Wagner. *Pseudo-Holomorphic Hamiltonian Systems and Kähler Duality of Complex Coadjoint Orbits*. University of Augsburg, 2024. Advisor(s): Kai Cieliebak. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03310) Location: Definition 2.3.9, pp.70–71; discussion p.76 and Outlook p.135.

**Literature check.** Status: The thesis leaves real dimension four open; no later resolution found by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.09480)


<a id="q3527"></a>

## Q3527. Relative h-principles for the remaining stable forms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 10.2.3) · **Collection** 36

For every oriented M, polyhedron A⊂M, q≥0, continuous α:D^q→H^p_dR(M), and F₀:D^q→Ω\_ρ(M) closed in class α on ∂D^q and near A, can F₀ be homotoped through Ω\_ρ(M), fixed on ∂D^q and near A, to F₁(s) closed with [F₁(s)]=α(s)?

**Context.** Advisors: Alexei Kovalev. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: The author’s Conjecture 10.2.3. Setup: Fix a stable-form type: positive G₂ 3-forms in dimension seven, or the Cartan 3-form ρ(x,y,z)=B(x,[y,z]) with B the Killing form on su(3), sl(3,ℝ), or su(1,2), or its metric Hodge-dual 5-form. Let Ω\_ρ(M) consist of forms pointwise equivalent by orientation-preserving linear maps to that model. Give it the smooth compact-open topology. Use the corresponding model dimension and form degree p.

**Source.** Laurence Hamilton Mayther. *Hitchin Functionals, h-Principles and Spectral Invariants*. University of Cambridge, 2023. Advisor(s): Alexei Kovalev. [primary source](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/c4ac0d9a-6c52-4303-a3da-d7af355de416) Location: Conjecture 10.2.3, pp.209–210; relative formulation in arXiv:2309.08721v2, p.1.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08721v2)

## Q3529. Asymptotic Toponogov rigidity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 36

Must the ideal triangle xyz∞ span a totally geodesic submanifold isometric to the model ideal triangle x₁y₁z₁∞ in H²(−1)?

**Context.** Degree: PhD in Mathematics, May 2016 Advisors: Alexander Borisenko. Source type: Dissertation-led paper follow-up Origin: The Blaschke Rolling Theorem in Riemannian Manifolds of Bounded Curvature, J. Geom. Anal. 36, 187 (2026). Setup: Let M be a Hadamard m-manifold with −1≤sec(M)<0. Let γ,γ₁ be unit-speed geodesic rays in M,H^m(−1), beginning at x,x₁ and ending at z∞,z₁∞. Set b(p)=lim_{t→∞}(t−d(p,γ(t))) and define b₁ similarly. Choose y,y₁ with d(x,y)=d(x₁,y₁), b(y)=b₁(y₁), and ∠(γ,xy)=∠(γ₁,x₁y₁).

**Source.** Kostiantyn Dmytrovych Drach. *The Blaschke Rolling Theorem in Riemannian Manifolds of Bounded Curvature*. B. Verkin Institute for Low Temperature Physics and Engineering, NAS of Ukraine, 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s12220-026-02436-7) Location: Section 6, Question 1, PDF pp.19–20.

**Literature check.** Status: Explicitly open in the inspected April 2026 journal version; no later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09.

## Q3530. An exact exponential drift asymptotic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.7.5) · **Collection** 36

Must lim_{t→∞}e^{-t}ℓ\_μ(R(q;t)) exist in (0,∞) for ν-almost every q?

**Context.** Advisors: Maxime Fortier Bourque, Vaibhav Gadre, Tara Brendle (repository-listed). Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: Question 6.1 of arXiv:2305.04627v1, retained in the revised dissertation. Setup: Let S be a closed oriented surface of genus≥2 and μ a probability on π₁(S), generating it as a semigroup and satisfying ∑\_g μ(g)|g|<∞ for a finite-generating-set word metric |·|. For iid μ-increments set Z_n=g₁⋯g_n and ℓ\_μ(X)=lim_n length_X(Z_n)/n, using geodesic free-homotopy length. Let ν be a measure on unit-area quadratic differentials Q¹(o). Suppose for each 0<ξ<1 some ε>0 makes ν-almost every unit-speed Teichmüller ray R(q;t) spend at least proportion ξ of [0,T] in the ε-thick part for all sufficiently large T.

**Source.** Aitor Azemar. *Asymptotic Geometric and Probabilistic Properties of Teichmüller Space*. University of Glasgow, 2024. Advisor(s): Maxime Fortier Bourque, Vaibhav Gadre, Tara Brendle (repository-listed). [primary source](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84802/1/2024AzemarPhD.pdf) Location: Question 4.7.5, p.68 / PDF p.75; hypothesis Theorem 4.5.7, p.63.

**Literature check.** Status: The author identifies this thesis chapter as the revised version of the 2023 preprint. No subsequent resolution located by 2026-10-09.

## Q3531. Almost-convex Cayley graphs for mapping class groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.4) · **Collection** 36

Does Mod_g admit a finite generating set whose Cayley graph is almost convex?

**Context.** Degree: PhD, 2022 Advisors: Mark Hagen. Source type: Dissertation-led paper follow-up Origin: Farb’s Questions 3.4–3.5 (2006), revisited by Petyt–Zalloum, Constructing metric spaces from systems of walls, following Theorem I. Setup: Call a metric space X almost convex if, for each k>0, some N(k)<∞ lets any x,y on a common sphere S_r(o), with d(x,y)=k, be joined inside B_r(o) by a path of length≤N(k), uniformly in o,r. Let Σ\_g be a closed oriented genus-g surface, g≥2, and Mod_g its orientation-preserving mapping class group.

**Source.** Harry Petyt and Abdul Zalloum. *Constructing metric spaces from systems of walls*. University of Bristol, 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00208-026-03534-1) Location: Final paper p.7, §1.2, following Theorem I; Farb preprint p.18, Question 3.4.

**Literature check.** Status: The inspected September 2026 final paper explicitly says its quasi-isometric almost-convex models do not answer these questions. No later resolution found, checked 2026-10-09.

<a id="q3532"></a>

## Q3532. Almost convexity of Teichmüller space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.5) · **Collection** 36

Is Teichmüller space T(Σ\_g), with its Teichmüller metric, almost convex?

**Context.** Degree: PhD, 2022 Advisors: Mark Hagen. Source type: Dissertation-led paper follow-up Origin: Farb’s Questions 3.4–3.5 (2006), revisited by Petyt–Zalloum, Constructing metric spaces from systems of walls, following Theorem I. Setup: Call a metric space X almost convex if, for each k>0, some N(k)<∞ lets any x,y on a common sphere S_r(o), with d(x,y)=k, be joined inside B_r(o) by a path of length≤N(k), uniformly in o,r. Let Σ\_g be a closed oriented genus-g surface, g≥2, and Mod_g its orientation-preserving mapping class group.

**Source.** Harry Petyt and Abdul Zalloum. *Constructing metric spaces from systems of walls*. University of Bristol, 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00208-026-03534-1) Location: Same final-paper paragraph, p.7; Farb preprint p.18, Question 3.5.

**Literature check.** Status: The inspected September 2026 final paper explicitly says its quasi-isometric almost-convex models do not answer these questions. No later resolution found, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://hpetyt.github.io/) · [2](https://hpetyt.github.io/thaeces.pdf) · [3](https://math.uchicago.edu/~farb/papers/mcgprobs.pdf)


<a id="q3533"></a>

## Q3533. Square-integrable half-pipe width

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Must ∫\_D w_φ² dA_H be finite?

**Context.** Degree: Doctorat in Mathematics; defended 4 April 2025 Advisors: Gérard Besson, Andrea Seppi. Source type: PhD dissertation and published follow-up Origin: Thesis conjecture following Proposition 1.2.7; repeated in Mean surfaces in half-pipe space and infinitesimal Teichmüller theory, JÉP 12 (2025). Setup: Let φ∈H^{3/2}(S¹;ℝ), D={z∈ℝ²:|z|<1}, Φ⁻(z)=sup{a(z):a affine, a|\_{S¹}≤φ}, and Φ⁺(z)=inf{a(z):a affine, a|\_{S¹}≥φ}. Set w_φ(z)=(Φ⁺(z)−Φ⁻(z))/√(1−|z|²). Equip D with the Klein hyperbolic area dA_H=(1−|z|²)^{-3/2}dxdy.

**Source.** Farid Diaf. *Mean surfaces in half-pipe space and infinitesimal Teichmüller theory*. Université Grenoble Alpes, 2025. [primary source](https://jep.centre-mersenne.org/item/10.5802/jep.311.pdf) Location: Thesis p.20; final paper Remark 7.10, p.1341; envelopes (21)–(22), p.1302.

**Literature check.** Status: Reaffirmed in the 2025 final paper. The inspected April and June 2026 follow-ups concern nonlinear minimal-Lagrangian/harmonic extensions; no resolution of this width criterion found by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/fariddiaf/home) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17804) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11141)

## Q3618. Hamiltonian volume minimality of the product torus

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 37

For n≥2, is Vol_n(φ(T^n))≥Vol_n(T^n) for every compactly supported Hamiltonian diffeomorphism φ of C^n?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière; Joaquim Serra (second supervisor, thesis acknowledgments). Origin: Oh’s older torus conjecture; the student’s current-regularity conjecture. Setup: Equip C^n with standard Euclidean and symplectic structures; T^n={(z_1,…,z_n):|z_i|=1}. A Hamiltonian diffeomorphism is a smooth time-dependent Hamiltonian flow’s time-one map. Also let H²=R^5_{x,y,t}, θ=dt−½Σ\_{j=1}²(x_jdy_j−y_jdx_j), with horizontal metric Σ(dx_j²+dy_j²). An integral two-current is Legendrian when its approximate tangent planes lie in ker θ. Regular means locally an integer multiple of an oriented smooth embedded surface.

**Source.** Gerard Orriols Giménez. *Variational problems for Lagrangian and Legendrian submanifolds*. ETH Zürich, 2025. Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière; Joaquim Serra. [primary source](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/bitstreams/218e70d8-d395-49df-8a0f-27ae17495932/download) Location: Thesis §1.4, Open Question 1, printed p.9.

**Literature check.** Status: Open in thesis and, for currents, July2026 final.

<a id="q3622"></a>

## Q3622. Optimal singular-set measure for weakly holomorphic maps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

For m≥3 and smooth projective algebraic N⊂CP^n with induced complex structure, must H^{2m−4}(Sing(u)∩K)<∞ for every compact K⊂M?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière. Origin: Caniato–Rivière’s 2021 questions; Pumberger’s 2008 thesis §2.8, p.56, already conjectured the smooth-domain tangent-uniqueness extension. Setup: Let M be a smooth 2m-manifold without boundary with Lipschitz almost-complex structure J. Let u∈W_loc^{1,2}(M,N) satisfy du∘J=J_N∘du almost everywhere and be locally strongly W^{1,2}-approximable by smooth N-valued maps. Sing(u) consists of points with positive density lim_{r↓0}r^{2−2m}∫\_{B_r(x)}|du|², using a local compatible metric. Tangent maps are weak W_loc^{1,2} limits of chart rescalings u(x+ρ·), ρ↓0.

**Source.** Riccardo Caniato. *Contributions to calibrated geometric analysis and analytical gauge theory in supercritical dimension*. ETH Zürich, 2023. Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière. [primary source](https://people.math.ethz.ch/~triviere/pdf/phd/caniato-thesis.pdf) Location: §2.1.4, p.10; bound (2.1.2), p.7.

**Literature check.** Status: Open in inspected thesis and authenticated accepted v2; Duke final metadata matches, final text uninspected. Checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.10371) · [2](https://people.math.ethz.ch/~riviere/pdf/phd/pumberger.pdf) · [3](https://sites.google.com/view/riccardocaniato) · [4](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/items/a264b361-2d93-423e-9b07-117ad9a20f3e)

<a id="q3631"></a>

## Q3631. Closed Hamiltonian-stationary surfaces with conical singularities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 37

Does some closed Riemann surface admit a weakly conformal Hamiltonian-stationary Lagrangian branched immersion into C² with at least one Schoen–Wolfson conical point?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière. Origin: Gaia–Orriols–Rivière construction; open closed-surface extension. Setup: Use standard C². A Lagrangian map, smooth outside finitely many branch/conical points, pulls back the standard symplectic form to zero and is immersed except at isolated branch or conical points. Hamiltonian stationary means stationary area under compactly supported Hamiltonian flows. A Schoen–Wolfson conical point has, up to unitary motion, tangent cone C_pq={r(√q e^(ipθ),i√p e^(−iqθ))/√(p+q):r≥0,θ∈R}, for coprime positive integers p≠q.

**Source.** Filippo Gaia. *Singularities of Hamiltonian stationary surfaces and related questions*. ETH Zürich, 2025. Advisor(s): Tristan Rivière. [primary source](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/server/api/core/bitstreams/87ac430b-ac9d-49f4-9e3c-e3e61b63ec08/content) Location: §2.1.1, Open Question 1, printed p.29.

**Literature check.** Status: Reaffirmed in the 2025 thesis; 2026 special-Lagrangian bridge examples have boundary.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/filippo-gaia) · [2](https://math.ethz.ch/news-and-events/news/d-math-news/2025/05/doctoral-exam-of-filippo-gaia.html)

## Q3721. CPC submanifolds in the solvable model

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

If S has the CPC property, can it be written S=H·o for some Iwasawa decomposition G=KAN and connected Lie subgroup H⊆AN?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Jürgen Berndt. Paper: Classification of cohomogeneity-one actions on symmetric spaces of noncompact type Origin: Authors’ closing question in §5. Setup: Let S be a connected homogeneous submanifold of a symmetric space M of noncompact type. Choose o∈S and write M=G/K with K the stabilizer of o. Homogeneous means an isometric Lie-group orbit. S has the CPC property if the spectra, including multiplicities, of its shape operators A_ξ are identical for all unit normal vectors ξ, even at different points.

**Source.** Ivan Solonenko and coauthors. *Classification of cohomogeneity-one actions on symmetric spaces of noncompact type*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.05553v2) Location: Open question, end of §5, p.37; CPC definition p.15.

## Q3722. Cusps in hyperbolic-product complements

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 38

**Context.** Advisor(s): Jean-François Lafont. Origin: Chapter 2 questions; joint project with Lafont, Miller and Minemyer. Setup: For m,n≥2, let M be a finite-volume Riemannian manifold universally covered by H^m×H^n. Put N=M\S; whenever it exists smoothly, let X_d be a d-fold ramified branched cover of M along S, d>2.

<a id="q3809"></a>

## Q3809. Which cycle types occur for hvh^{-1}v^{-1} when Singer cycles h,v generate PGL(d,q)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 39

Which cycle types occur for hvh^{-1}v^{-1} when Singer cycles h,v generate PGL(d,q)?

**Context.** Authors: Tarik Aougab, Adam Friedman-Brown, Luke Jeffreys, Jiajie Ma. Paper: On the monodromy and spin parity of single-cylinder origamis in the minimal stratum. These are post-thesis paper questions. Set n=(q^d−1)/(q−1), d≥2, q a prime power. A Singer cycle is an element of PGL(d,q) acting as an n-cycle on PG(d−1,q). For an origami, square monodromy is generated by its right/up gluing permutations; primitive means this action is primitive. A minimal [1,1]-origami in H(2g−2) uses 2g−1 unit squares and one horizontal/vertical cylinder. With div(ω)=(2g−2)P, spin parity is h⁰(X,O((g−1)P)) modulo 2. Locators: Questions 9.6–9.7, pp.45–46, inspected arXiv:2502.09498v2 (2026-01-25).

**Source.** Tarik Aougab, Adam Friedman-Brown, Luke Jeffreys and Jiajie Ma. *On the monodromy and spin parity of single-cylinder origamis in the minimal stratum*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09498v2) Location: Questions 9.6–9.7, pp.45–46, inspected arXiv:2502.09498v2 (2026-01-25).

**Literature check.** Status, 2026-10-10: no resolution found in bounded searches.

## Q3814. For Lipschitz f_i:Q→R and π\_i:Q→R^d, assume Σ\_i||f_i||\_L∏\_{j=1}^d Lip(π\_i^j)<∞. Does Σ\_i f_i…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 39

For Lipschitz f_i:Q→R and π\_i:Q→R^d, assume Σ\_i||f_i||\_L∏\_{j=1}^d Lip(π\_i^j)<∞. Does Σ\_i f_i det(Dπ\_i)=0 almost everywhere imply Σ\_i T(f_i,π\_i^1,…,π\_i^d)=0 for every such T?

**Context.** Let d≥1, Q=[0,1]^d and ||f||\_L=max(||f||∞,Lip(f)). A Lang metric d-current is a real multilinear T:Lip(Q)^{d+1}→R, continuous for pointwise-convergent tuples with uniformly bounded Lipschitz constants, and vanishing if any π-coordinate is constant near supp(f). Finite mass is not required.

**Source.** Jakub Takáč. *The structure of rectifiable and purely unrectifiable metric spaces; the infinite-mass Flat Chain Conjecture and prescribed Jacobians*. University of Warwick, 2025. Advisor(s): David Bate. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/200303/) Location: the injectivity question for potential forms, equivalently stated using a zero relation. Thesis Definition 4.3.7 and following paragraph, p.134; Takáč, Failure of Lang’s Flat Chain Conjecture and non-regularity of the prescribed Jacobian equation, Definition 3.7, p.19.

**Literature check.** Status, 10 October 2026: thesis and arXiv:2506.13718v2 (26 November 2025) independently inspected; no later resolution found. The case d=1 is settled; higher dimensions remain the target. Submission alone does not establish degree conferral.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13718v2)

## Q3835. Twisted growth versus ordinary conjugacy growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 9.3.1) · **Collection** 39

Must c_φ≺c hold for every endomorphism φ of G?

**Context.** Origin: Thesis Conjecture 9.3.1. Setup: Let G be finitely presented and S a finite generating set with positive weights on S∪S⁻¹. For φ∈End(G), declare x∼\_φ y when x=gyφ(g)^(−1) for some g∈G. Let c_φ(r) count these classes meeting the weighted word ball of radius r, and c(r) count ordinary conjugacy classes there. Write f≺h if f(r)≤a h(ar+b)+b for all r≥0, with a>0,b≥0. Sources:

**Source.** Maarten Lathouwers. *An algebraic and geometric approach to infinite Reidemeister numbers*. KU Leuven, 2025. Advisor(s): Karel Dekimpe. [primary source](https://lirias.kuleuven.be/4233981) Location: p. 133, Conjecture 9.3.1; p. 40, Definition 4.3.3

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 10 October 2026: no general resolution found. Virtually abelian endomorphisms and generalized-Heisenberg automorphisms are known cases.

## Q3877. Must g_ε=g₀ for all sufficiently small ε?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 39

Must g_ε=g₀ for all sufficiently small ε?

**Context.** Write T=R/Z. Consider a smooth family of Riemannian metrics on T², g_ε=[1+f₁(x₁)+f₂(x₂)+εU(x₁,x₂,ε)\](dx₁²+dx₂²), where f₁,f₂∈C∞(T) each have exactly two critical points, U is smooth, and the conformal factor is positive. Suppose every g_ε is invariant under (x₁,x₂)↦(1−x₁,x₂) and (x₁,x₂)↦(x₁,1−x₂). Assume the set of lengths of all closed g_ε-geodesics is independent of ε.

**Source.** Yunzhe Li. *Spectral Rigidity and Nonrigidity of Dynamical Systems*. Institute of Science and Technology Austria, 2026. Advisor(s): Vadim Kaloshin. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/22255) Location: Explicit thesis Question 2, §1.2.4, p.13; no earlier exact formulation located.

## Q3888. Orbifold type detected by virtual retractions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 39

Setup: Let f∈C(x) have degree≥2 and finite postcritical set P_f. Write IMG(f) for the monodromy image of π₁(P¹∖P_f) on the iterated-preimage tree based outside P_f. Property (LR) means every finitely generated H≤G is a retract of some finite-index K≤G containing H. For the canonical Thurston orbifold, ν(z)=lcm{deg_w(fᵐ): m≥1, fᵐ(w)=z}, allowing ∞; χ=2−Σ\_{z∈P_f}(1−1/ν(z)), with 1/∞=0. Sources:

**Source.** Jorge Fariña-Asategui. *Groups acting on rooted trees: Dimension, subgroups, randomness and dynamics*. Lund University and University of the Basque Country, 2026. Advisor(s): Anitha Thillaisundaram and Gustavo A. Fernández-Alcober. [primary source](https://lup.lub.lu.se/search/files/248603771/kappa-farina.pdf) Location: Thesis pp.35–36; orbifold definition: Bonk–Meyer (2020), §2.5, p.502.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 10 October 2026: quadratic polynomials are settled; no general resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16869v1) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40598-020-00156-6)

## Q3956. Does every pants graph of a finite-type surface admit exactly one coarse median structure?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 40

Does every pants graph of a finite-type surface admit exactly one coarse median structure?

**Context.** A coarse median on (X,d) is a symmetric map μ:X³→X with μ(x,x,y)=x and, for some C≥1, d(μ(μ(x,p,y),p,z),μ(x,p,μ(y,p,z)))≤C and d(μ(x,y,z),μ(p,y,z))≤C d(x,p)+C for all x,y,z,p. Two such maps define the same structure when their pointwise distances are uniformly bounded. Vertices are isotopy classes of pants decompositions; an edge replaces one curve within a one-holed torus or four-holed sphere by a curve intersecting it minimally, once or twice respectively. Edges have length one.

**Source.** Elia Fioravanti and Alessandro Sisto. *On uniqueness of coarse median structures*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.2140/agt.2026.26.2689) Location: final Question and Problem, p.2690; definitions §2.2.

**Literature check.** Status: These are paper-origin questions. Uniqueness from cocompact cubulations alone is weaker. No matching later resolution found, 10 October 2026.

## Q3965. Let closed Riemannian n-manifolds M_i converge in Gromov–Hausdorff distance to a compact metric…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 0.7) · **Collection** 40

Let closed Riemannian n-manifolds M_i converge in Gromov–Hausdorff distance to a compact metric space X with 0&lt;diam(X)<∞. Suppose vol(M_i)→0, sec(M_i)≥−1, and every M_i is aspherical with π₁(M_i) of type As. Must X be a topological aspherical manifold without boundary?

**Context.** A manifold is aspherical if its universal cover is contractible. A finitely generated group Γ has type As if, for every normal virtually nilpotent subgroup Λ◁Γ and every finite subgroup F≤Γ/Λ, the normalizer of F has finite index in Γ/Λ. Virtually nilpotent means having a nilpotent subgroup of finite index.

**Source.** Xiaochun Rong. *Gromov–Hausdorff limits of aspherical manifolds*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.2140/gt.2026.30.2685) Location: final Conjecture 0.7, sectional-curvature branch, p.2688; Definition 0.4, p.2686.

For all integers p,q,r≥2 with 1/p+1/q+1/r<1, does Δ(p,q,r)=⟨x,y | x^p=y^q=(xy)^r=1⟩ satisfy girth₂(Δ(p,q,r))=∞?

**Source.** Pratyush Mishra. *Infinite girth and profinite properties of residually finite groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10925) Location: Remark 4.9, p.10.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no matching resolution found. Ordinary infinite girth of these triangle groups does not establish the two-generator bound.

## Q4059. Does every complete finite-volume real-hyperbolic n-manifold, n≥2, have a finite-sheeted cover…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 27) · **Collection** 41

Does every complete finite-volume real-hyperbolic n-manifold, n≥2, have a finite-sheeted cover whose compact core admits a perfect circle-valued Morse function?

**Context.** For a complete finite-volume real-hyperbolic manifold M, use its compact core M\* obtained by truncating cusps, or M\*=M when closed. A circle-valued Morse function is smooth f:M\*→S¹, has nondegenerate critical points, and has no critical points on its boundary restriction. It is perfect when it has exactly |χ(M\*)| critical points.

**Source.** Ludovico Battista and Bruno Martelli. *Hyperbolic 4-manifolds with perfect circle-valued Morse functions*. 2022. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1090/tran/8542) Location: Question 27; restated in Italiano–Migliorini, arXiv:2503.24128v1, Question 8.3, p.29.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no general resolution found. Known four-, five- and six-dimensional examples do not prove this universal assertion. Odd-dimensional virtual fibering and single-dimensional existence are not counted separately.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.24128)

## Q4060. Is inf_{M∈M(X)}V_R(M) attained by some member of M(X)? Topology varies while conformal infinity…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.1.3) · **Collection** 41

Is inf_{M∈M(X)}V_R(M) attained by some member of M(X)? Topology varies while conformal infinity X stays fixed.

**Context.** Let X be a nonempty compact Riemann surface with components of genus≥2, and M(X) the complete oriented convex-cocompact hyperbolic 3-manifolds, possibly disconnected, with conformal infinity X. Convex-cocompact means compact convex core. Normalize V_R(M)=Vol(C_r)−½∫\_{∂C_r}H dA+πrχ(X), where C_r exhausts M by equidistant convex boundaries, H is half the outward shape-operator trace, and their induced metrics satisfy 4e^{-2r}g_r→h, the curvature−1 metric on X.

**Source.** Viola Giovannini. *On the Renormalized Volume of Hyperbolic 3-Manifolds with Compressible Boundary*. University of Luxembourg and University of Pisa, 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07598v2) Location: Thesis Question 2.1.3, p.42; use the compact-boundary formulation of Cremaschi–Giovannini–Schlenker, Journal of Geometry and Physics 217 (2025), 105628, Question 1.3, already in their 2024 preprint.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no resolution found. Fixed-topology results allowing the conformal boundary to vary do not answer this question.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.geomphys.2025.105628)

## Q4061. Let M be a complete finite-volume real-hyperbolic n-manifold, n≥4, and f:M→S¹ a homotopically…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 24) · **Collection** 41

Let M be a complete finite-volume real-hyperbolic n-manifold, n≥4, and f:M→S¹ a homotopically nontrivial smooth map with finitely generated H=ker(f_\*:π₁M→Z). Must the covering M_H associated to H be infinitesimally rigid? Precisely, for its holonomy ρ:H→Isom(ℍⁿ), must H¹(H,𝔤\_{Adρ})=0, where 𝔤 is the Lie algebra of Isom(ℍⁿ) and h∈H acts by Ad(ρ(h))? This quotient of infinitesimal representation deformations by infinitesimal conjugations is the source’s rigidity notion.

**Source.** Ludovico Battista. *Infinitesimal rigidity for cubulated manifolds*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10711-022-00765-9) Location: Conjecture 24, p.29; paper-origin.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no general resolution found. The paper proves particular four- and five-dimensional examples, not the universal conjecture.


<a id="q4066"></a>

## Q4066. If G and H are quasi-isometric, must they be isomorphic as Lie groups?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 19.113) · **Collection** 41

If G and H are quasi-isometric, must they be isomorphic as Lie groups?

**Context.** Let G,H be connected simply-connected solvable real Lie groups such that every adjoint operator ad(X) on either Lie algebra has real eigenvalues. Equip them with left-invariant Riemannian metrics. A quasi-isometry is a map f:G→H with coarsely dense image and constants A≥1,B≥0 satisfying A⁻¹d(x,y)−B≤d(fx,fy)≤Ad(x,y)+B.

**Source.** Yves Cornulier. *On the quasi-isometric classification of locally compact groups*. [primary source](https://www.normalesup.org/~cornulier/qihlc.pdf) Location: Yves Cornulier’s Conjecture 19.113, On the quasi-isometric classification of locally compact groups, §19.6.6,p.45 of author version, restated as Tom Ferragut’s Conjecture 0.1, AGT 26 (2026),p.865.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no general resolution found. Known classifications of special families, and recognition of virtually polycyclic groups, do not classify all these Lie groups.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.2140/agt.2026.26.863)


<a id="q4090"></a>

## Q4090. For every integer d≥3 and every compact convex K⊂Rᵈ with nonempty interior, does int(K) admit a…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 41

For every integer d≥3 and every compact convex K⊂Rᵈ with nonempty interior, does int(K) admit a recursive (d−1)-skeleton?

**Context.** A recursive Λ-cell in R is a bounded open interval. In Rᵏ, k≥2, it has form {x:πₑx∈C₋, β(πₑx)&lt;xₑ<τ(πₑx)}, where πₑ deletes one coordinate, C₋ is a recursive Λ-cell in Rᵏ⁻¹, and β,τ:Rᵏ⁻¹→R are globally Λ-Lipschitz for the norm ||y||∞=maxᵢ|yᵢ|, with β<τ on C₋. A recursive Λ-skeleton is a finite family of pairwise disjoint such cells whose union differs from the specified set by a Lebesgue-null set. Only coordinate choices and permutations are allowed, with no rotations.

**Source.** Ahmadreza Azimifard. *Plunge bounds for Fourier concentration operators: geometry, smoothing, and wave packets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09406v1) Location: Open Questions 19.21, p.50.

**Literature check.** Status: Paper-origin. The source reports this bound for balls and standard simplices, but not arbitrary convex bodies. Mere existence with some finite slope, the polytope-only case, and separate dimensional subcases are not counted. No matching resolution found, 10 October 2026.


<a id="q4096"></a>

## Q4096. For every pair of integers n≥5 and 3≤j≤n−2, if every sum of n−j eigenvalues of D²ζ(u) is…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 41

For every pair of integers n≥5 and 3≤j≤n−2, if every sum of n−j eigenvalues of D²ζ(u) is nonnegative for every u∈Sⁿ⁻¹, must Fζ((1−t)K+tL)≥Fζ(K)^(1−t)Fζ(L)^t hold for all convex bodies K,L and 0&lt;t<1?

**Context.** A convex body is any nonempty compact convex subset of Rⁿ. Its support function is h_K(u)=supₓ∈K x·u. The mixed volume V(K₁,…,Kₙ) is the coefficient of t₁⋯tₙ in volₙ(ΣtᵢKᵢ), divided by n!. Brackets indicate repeated arguments; B is the unit ball. For ζ∈C²(Sⁿ⁻¹), choose convex bodies A,C with ζ=h_A−h_C and set Fζ(K)=V(K[j],A,B[n−j−1])−V(K[j],C,B[n−j−1]); this is independent of the choice. Let D²ζ(u) be the Hessian of x↦|x|ζ(x/|x|), restricted to u⊥.

**Source.** Leo Brauner and Oscar Ortega-Moreno. *Hyperbolicity Beyond Convexity in the Alexandrov–Fenchel Inequality*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08746v1) Location: after Theorem C, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status: Paper-origin residual. The hypothesis ensures Fζ≥0; it does not require ζ≥0. Earlier Colesanti–Hug–Saorín-Gómez work gives necessary conditions. No matching resolution found, 10 October 2026.

## Q4135. Does λ₁^α(Ω)≤λ₁^α(B) hold for every α<0 whenever Ω is convex with d≥2, or simply connected with…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 42

Does λ₁^α(Ω)≤λ₁^α(B) hold for every α<0 whenever Ω is convex with d≥2, or simply connected with d=2, and B is a ball with |B|=|Ω|?

**Context.** For a bounded Lipschitz domain Ω⊂Rᵈ and α<0, define its first Robin eigenvalue by λ₁^α(Ω)=inf₀≠u∈H¹(Ω) (∫Ω|∇u|² dx+α∫∂Ω|u|² dS)/(∫Ω|u|² dx), using the boundary trace and surface measure dS. A domain is open, nonempty and connected.

**Source.** David Krejčiřík. *Spectral geometry: old questions and new answers*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28602v1) Location: Open Problems 3–4, pp. 64–65.

**Literature check.** Status: These are survey-recorded residual problems, not thesis-origin claims. The first restricts Bareket’s false unrestricted-volume conjecture to the stated classes. The second removes convexity from the known fixed-surface-area comparison. The 2026 small-coupling result does not cover all α<0; the newer torsional-rigidity final concerns a different functional. No matching resolution found, 10 October 2026.

## Q4178. If a hyperbolic group G regularly embeds into a hyperbolic group H, must Confdim(∂∞G)≤Confdim(∂∞H)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.5) · **Collection** 42

If a hyperbolic group G regularly embeds into a hyperbolic group H, must Confdim(∂∞G)≤Confdim(∂∞H)?

**Context.** A regular embedding between finitely generated groups is a map between their Cayley-graph vertex sets that is C-Lipschitz and has every fiber of size at most C, for some C. Confdim denotes Ahlfors-regular conformal dimension: the infimum of Hausdorff dimensions of Ahlfors-regular metric spaces quasisymmetrically equivalent to the boundary with a visual metric.

**Source.** David Hume and John M. Mackay. *Connecting conformal dimension and Poincaré profiles*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10469v1) Location: Question 1.5, p. 4; definitions pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: Current arXiv and author listings retain a preprint. The paper proves several special cases; no general resolution was found.


<a id="q4181"></a>

## Q4181. For each marked conformal structure c on S, is there a unique pair (E,Σ), up to marked isometry,…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.10) · **Collection** 42

For each marked conformal structure c on S, is there a unique pair (E,Σ), up to marked isometry, where E is a hyperbolic end with conformal infinity c and Σ is a convex ideal polyhedral surface of combinatorics Γ and exterior dihedral angles θ?

**Context.** Let S be a closed oriented surface of genus at least two, Γ the embedded 1-skeleton of a cell decomposition, and θ:E(Γ)→(0,π). Assume the incident θ-values sum to 2π at each vertex, and their sum exceeds 2π along every null-homotopic non-backtracking closed edge path in the dual graph Γ\* that does not bound a face. Dual edges inherit their corresponding primal-edge weights. A hyperbolic end is diffeomorphic to S×(0,∞), complete toward infinity, with metric completion adding a concave pleated boundary.

**Source.** Jean-Marc Schlenker. *Equivariant isometric immersions of surfaces in hyperbolic space*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32300v1) Location: Question 1.10, p. 5; §1.3.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: This remains a question in the current paper; no later resolution was found.


<a id="q4183"></a>

## Q4183. Does 2V have property (T)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3) · **Collection** 42

Does 2V have property (T)?

**Context.** Let C={0,1}^N. The group 2V consists of homeomorphisms of C² given by finite coordinatewise prefix replacements between partitions into product cylinders. Kazhdan's property (T) means that every unitary representation with almost invariant unit vectors has a nonzero invariant vector.

**Source.** Roman Sauer and Eduard Schesler. *Higher Brin–Thompson groups are Kazhdan*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09524v1) Location: Question 1.3, p. 3; definitions pp. 2, 14–15.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: The current paper proves property (T) for nV only for sufficiently large n and explicitly leaves 2V open. No later resolution was found.


<a id="q4184"></a>

## Q4184. For every ε>0, does M₂ contain a nonempty open subset U such that every degree-two cover of…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 42

For every ε>0, does M₂ contain a nonempty open subset U such that every degree-two cover of every X∈U has a new eigenvalue in (0,ε)? Covers here may be disconnected, as in the source's convention.

**Context.** Surfaces are complete, connected, orientable and hyperbolic, with finite area. For a degree-two cover Y→X, its new spectrum is the Laplacian spectrum on L²-functions odd under the deck involution; λ₁ⁿᵉʷ(Y) is its infimum. Write λ₁(X) for the first nonzero spectral value, not necessarily an eigenvalue. M₂ denotes the moduli space of closed genus-two surfaces, with its Fenchel–Nielsen topology.

**Source.** Lawford Hatcher and Bram Petri. *Spectral gaps of double covers of hyperbolic surfaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07193v1) Location: Questions 1.5(c) and 1.6, p. 3; definitions pp. 2, 7.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: The paper leaves both questions open after disproving the unrestricted 1/4 bound. No later resolution was found.
